Math Research Digest

**Grounded | July 6–12, 2026** What a week to be paying attention. Two results stand out for their audacity alone: the Burau representation of the 4-strand braid group is faithful, settling a question that has resisted resolution for decades and immediately implying faithfulness of the related Jones representation at this rank; and the Montgomery-Yang problem — whether a rational homology projective plane with isolated quotient singularities can have more than three singular points — is completely resolved in the negative, with the proof reaching across from gauge theory to lattice combinatorics in a genuinely surprising way. Both results have the character of problems where the difficulty was less about missing machinery than about finding the right angle of attack. A second theme running through this week's papers is universality — the phenomenon where wildly different systems collapse to the same limiting behavior — appearing in at least three distinct settings simultaneously. Random matrix universality gets a conceptual unification: the Sine_β and Airy_β processes are now characterized as the *unique* solutions to loop equation hierarchies, meaning any ensemble approximately satisfying those equations must converge to the universal limits. Separately, a striking double universality result for large-N melonic tensor models shows that any tensor integral, regardless of its detailed structure, reduces to the same class of Feynman diagrams — a result with immediate computational payoffs in combin

Number Theory

30

Reduction modulo p of crystalline Galois representations via μ_p-equivariance

Bhargav Bhatt, Toby Gee, Mark Kisin

Crystalline Galois representations are p-adic symmetry groups of number fields with especially nice structure, and reducing them mod p produces simpler but subtler objects. This paper derives new constraints on those mod-p reductions and proves the 'elimination direction' of an explicit Serre weight conjecture—predicting which mod-p representations appear—for general reductive groups over Q_p using cutting-edge prismatic cohomology methods.

math.NT math.AG
Original abstract

For a crystalline representation of the absolute Galois group of Q_p, with given Hodge-Tate weights, we obtain new constraints on the inertial weights of its mod p reduction. This allows us to formulate an explicit Serre weight conjecture, in the generality of L-parameters for unramified connected reductive groups over Q_p, and to prove the elimination direction of this conjecture. The proof uses prismatic techniques to show that the reductions modulo p of the Breuil-Kisin modules attached to crystalline Galois representations acquire a natural μ_p-equivariant structure. Combining this with results on the geometry of the μ_p-fixed points of affine Grassmannians leads to our new constraint.

A higher Gross-Zagier type formula for moduli of shtukas at deeper level

Patrick Bieker

The Gross-Zagier formula famously links derivatives of L-functions to heights of special points on modular curves; Yun and Zhang proved a function-field analogue involving higher derivatives and 'shtukas' (geometric objects over finite fields). This paper extends their formula beyond square-free level, requiring new analysis of the geometry and cohomology of integral models of schtuka moduli spaces at deeper level—a significant step toward the full arithmetic theory of these L-functions.

math.NT math.AG
Original abstract

We generalize the function field analogue analogue of the Gross-Zagier and Waldspurger formulae due to Yun and Zhang relating the (higher) derivative of an automorphic (base change) L-function to intersection numbers of Heegner-Drinfeld cycles on moduli spaces of shtukas to not necessarily square-free level. The main new input is the study of the geometry and cohomology of the appropriate ``integral models'' of moduli spaces of shtukas at deeper level.

$p$-adic Maass--Shimura operators on $μ$-ordinary Igusa varieties

Andrew Graham, Pol van Hoften, Sean Howe

Maass–Shimura operators are differential operators on automorphic forms attached to Shimura varieties, arithmetic objects generalizing modular curves. This paper extends these operators p-adically to μ-ordinary Igusa varieties—special integral models useful for p-adic interpolation—and integrates the rank-one operators into a formal group action. The payoff is a new p-adic interpolation of automorphic forms in a broader context than previously achieved.

math.NT math.AG math.RT
Original abstract

For Shimura varieties of Hodge type, we optimally extend algebraic Maass--Shimura differential operators on $p$-integral nearly holomorphic automorphic forms to differential operators on $μ$-ordinary Mantovan Igusa varieties. We then show that the rank one operators can be integrated to an action of an explicit formal group. Via $p$-adic Fourier theory, this provides a $p$-adic interpolation by extending the action of a symmetric algebra of differential operators to the algebra of functions on the Tate module of the dual $p$-divisible group. Passing to the generic fiber, we obtain an action of an explicit algebra of $p$-adic locally analytic functions, and we show that the action of the subalgebra of locally constant functions is equivalent to a natural Hecke action and thus preserves classical forms. In the ordinary case, we further show that the locally analytic action extends to nearly overconvergent automorphic forms. Our results extend, clarify, and recover prior constructions.

On dynamical irreducible set of polynomials

Himanshu Sharma

A set of polynomials over a finite field is dynamically irreducible if repeatedly composing them always yields irreducible polynomials—ones that can't be factored. The paper gives the first explicit constructions of such sets over finite fields of the form F_{p^p} and proves large dynamically irreducible sets exist over every field of prime-power order. These results advance the arithmetic of iterated polynomial maps, which underpins pseudorandom number generation and certain cryptographic protocols.

math.NT
Original abstract

In this article, a necessary and sufficient condition is proved for the dynamical irreducibility of a family of polynomials over a finite field. Using this result, an explicit construction of a dynamically irreducible set of polynomials is given over the finite field $\mathbb{F}_{p^p}$ where $p$ is a prime. Moreover, the existence of dynamically irreducible sets of size at least $p^2$ is also established over every finite field $\mathbb{F}_q$ where $q$ is a $p$ power. Finally, a bound on the cardinality of the set is given that needs to be tested for the dynamical irreducibility of a set of polynomials.

A proof of Andrews-El Bachraoui's conjecture on the parity of coefficients of a $q$-series

Eric H. Liu, Ernest X. W. Xia

math.NT
Original abstract

Recently, Andrews and El Bachraoui studied a partition function $s_1(n)$ which counts the number of two-color partitions into distinct parts of $n$ whose smallest part occurs in one prescribed color only, while every larger part may occur in either color or in both colors. They obtained a complete description modulo 4 for $s_1(n)$. They also considered a $q$-series $T_{o}(q)$ which is the odd companion series of the generating function for $s_1(n)$. At the end of their paper, they presented a conjecture on the parity of the coefficients of $T_o(q)$. In this paper, we confirm this conjecture. Moreover,we establish an infinite family of congruences modulo 8 for the coefficients of $S_1(q)$ and prove that $s_1(n)/8$ takes integer values with natural density 1 for $n\geq 0$.

Ideal class monoids of cubic orders

Sungmun Cho, Jungtaek Hong, Yuchan Lee

An order in a number field is a ring sitting inside it that may lack the nice properties of the full ring of integers; its ideal class monoid tracks how ideals multiply and fail to be principal. For cubic orders satisfying a condition called Gorenstein, this paper gives an explicit Euler product formula — an infinite product over primes — counting the 'genus' of fractional ideals, a measure of how far the order is from having unique factorization. This generalizes classical genus theory from quadratic to cubic fields.

math.NT
Original abstract

Let $R$ be an order in a number field, let $\overline{\mathrm{Cl}}(R)$ be its ideal class monoid, and let $\mathrm{Cl}(R)$ act on it by multiplication. The local-global product formula identifies the orbit set $\mathrm{Cl}(R)\backslash\overline{\mathrm{Cl}}(R)$ with a product of local orbit sets; in this sense, it is the genus set of fractional $R$-ideals. For a Gorenstein order $R$ in a cubic extension of number fields, we give a closed Euler product formula for the cardinality of this genus set. The local factors come from an explicit classification of local cubic overorders: for arbitrary local cubic orders, we parametrize all overorders, determine their inclusion relations, and identify the Gorenstein ones. As an application to Bhargava's parametrization of $2\times3\times3$ cubes, our formula gives the exact number of $\mathrm{Cl}(R)$-equivalence classes of integral $\mathrm{GL}_2(\mathbb Z)\times\mathrm{SL}_3(\mathbb Z)\times\mathrm{SL}_3(\mathbb Z)$-orbits whose associated cubic ring is the prescribed Gorenstein order $R$.

Global geometrization of local smooth integral models in the Hitchin fibration for $\mathrm{GL}_n$: The Bass case

Sungmun Cho, Jungtaek Hong

Orbital integrals—key quantities in the Langlands program connecting number theory and geometry—are notoriously hard to compute locally. This paper lifts a local smoothing technique for GL_n into the global framework of the Hitchin fibration (a geometric tool over moduli of curves), producing an explicit closed formula for the ℓ-adic cohomology of compactified Jacobians of spectral curves in the 'Bass case,' a combinatorial class of singularities where the algebra is manageable.

math.NT math.AG
Original abstract

In previous joint work, we proposed a new method to study local orbital integrals for $\mathrm{GL}_n$ (where $n=3$ or in the Bass case), by employing a smoothening method of a certain scheme defined over a henselian ring. In this paper, we geometrize this local smoothening method within the framework of the global Hitchin fibration for $\mathrm{GL}_n$ in the Bass case. Consequently, we provide a closed formula for the $\ell$-adic cohomology of the compactified Jacobian of a spectral curve over a finite field with double singularities whose local rings are integral domains, provided that the normalization of a spectral curve is isomorphic to $\mathbb{P}^1$.

Mordell Curves with Ordinates in Arithmetic Progression

Sakha A. Alkabouss, Benjamin Earp-Lynch, Simon Earp-Lynch et al.

A Mordell curve is an elliptic curve of the form y² = x³ + k, and this paper asks how long an arithmetic progression the y-coordinates of rational points can form. The authors show length-7 progressions haven't been ruled out, construct explicit infinite families achieving length 6, and build parametric families of elliptic curves with points in arithmetic progression — advancing a concrete open problem in Diophantine geometry.

math.NT
Original abstract

We show that Mordell curves with arithmetic progressions in the $y$-coordinate of length $7$ have not been ruled out by previous work, and we give non-isomorphic families of Mordell curves with $y$-arithmetic progressions of length $6$. We also construct parametric families of elliptic curves of moderate rank, with subfamilies possessing rational points in arithmetic progression.

$p$-adic Hahn series with sparse support

Shanwen Wang, Yijun Yuan

P-adic Hahn series generalize power series by allowing exponents from ordered groups, and this paper asks when such series are transcendental over the p-adic numbers. The authors show that a 'sparseness' condition on which exponents appear forces transcendence, and use this to prove a conjecture about the order-type structure of algebraic p-adic Hahn series with finitely many accumulation points in their support.

math.NT math.CO
Original abstract

Let $p$ be a prime number. We introduce a sparseness condition on the supports of $p$-adic Hahn series, and prove that this condition implies transcendence over $\breve{\mathbf Q}_p$, the completed maximal unramified extension of $\mathbf{Q}_p$. As an application, we prove the order-type conjecture of $\mathbf{Q}_p$-algebraic $p$-adic Hahn series with bounded support under the condition that the support has only finitely many accumulation points. All results in this paper have been fully formalized in the Lean theorem prover (v 4.31.0), building over Mathlib.

Schwarz maps for modular curves

Yaacov Kopeliovich, Camilo Sanabria Malagón

Modular curves are Riemann surfaces encoding deep number-theoretic symmetry, and Klein and Hurwitz asked how to associate explicit differential equations to modular transformations of given degree N. This paper constructs canonical models of these curves and the corresponding linear ODEs with rational coefficients—called Schwarz maps—whose solutions encode the modular symmetry. This settles a classical 19th-century question and provides new tools connecting differential equations and arithmetic geometry.

math.NT math.AG math.CV
Original abstract

We solve a classical problem posed by F. Klein and studied by A. Hurwitz concerning the construction of linear ordinary differential equations associated with modular transformations of fixed degree. For every odd integer $N\ge 3$ (respectively, even integer $N\ge 4$), we construct a canonical invariant model of the modular curve $X(N)=\mathbb{H}/Γ(N)$ (respectively, $X_H(N)=\mathbb{H}/H(N)$ where $H(N)=Γ(N)\capΓ_0^0(2N)$), together with a linear ordinary differential equation with rational coefficients whose Schwarz map parametrizes this model and whose projective monodromy group is the finite quotient $PSL_2(\mathbb{Z})/\tildeΓ(N)$ (respectively, $PSL_2(\mathbb{Z})/\tilde{H}(N)$). The construction is expressed in terms of invariant projective geometry and Picard-Vessiot theory and yields equations that are canonical up to projective equivalence. In this framework, Hurwitz's classical equation for degree $7$ appears as a special case of a general mechanism. The results place Klein's question within the modern theory of algebraic linear ordinary differential equations and provide a uniform geometric realization of modular transformation groups as projective differential Galois groups. As an application, we construct an explicit example of a linear ordinary differential equation associated with $X(9)$.

A Conditional Refinement of Page's Theorem on zeros of Dirichlet $L$-functions

Debmalya Basak, Kyle Pratt

Dirichlet L-functions are complex-analytic tools central to prime number theory, and 'Landau-Siegel zeros' are suspected-but-unproven zeros dangerously close to s=1 that would sabotage prime distribution estimates. Page's theorem says at most one such zero exists across all L-functions with conductor ≤Q. This paper shows that assuming all complex zeros avoid a shrinking neighborhood of s=1, Page's theorem sharpens dramatically—pushing the boundaries of what's conditionally provable about these elusive zeros.

math.NT
Original abstract

Landau--Siegel zeros are hypothetical zeros of Dirichlet $L$-functions that are close to the point $s=1$. A classic theorem of Page shows at most one such zero can exist among all Dirichlet $L$-functions with conductor $\leq Q$. We show that one can significantly refine Page's theorem under the assumption that all non-real zeros of Dirichlet $L$-functions lie outside a shrinking neighborhood of $s=1$.

Random Multiplicative Functions and Making Squares from Polynomial Values

Régis de la Bretèche, Victor Y. Wang, Max Wenqiang Xu

A Rademacher multiplicative function assigns ±1 to primes randomly, and the paper asks how the sum of f evaluated along polynomial values behaves statistically. The authors prove central limit theorems for these sums by showing that the equation P(n₁)P(n₂)P(n₃)P(n₄)=perfect square has surprisingly few solutions—a 'paucity' result—which controls correlations that would otherwise obstruct Gaussian behavior.

math.NT math.PR
Original abstract

For a large family of polynomials $P(X)\in \mathbb{Z}[X]$, we prove central limit theorems for $\sum_{n\le N} f(P(n))$ for both Rademacher and extended Rademacher multiplicative functions $f$. To achieve this, we establish a paucity phenomenon in counting solutions to \[P(n_1)P(n_2)P(n_3)P(n_4) = \square, \quad 1\le n_1, n_2, n_3, n_4 \le N.\] Results of Hooley, Evertse--Silverman, and Reuss play an important role in the proof. Our estimates are sharpest for $°P = 2$, thanks to the rich theory of Pell--Fermat equations.

Small sums of roots of unity

Alexandros Kalogirou

How small can a nonzero sum of k terms, each an N-th root of unity (complex numbers on the unit circle at rational angles), actually be? This paper establishes upper bounds of the form N^(−αk) where αk grows with k, meaning such sums can be surprisingly tiny. Understanding these minimum magnitudes connects to problems in number theory and combinatorics about cancellation in exponential sums.

math.NT
Original abstract

We address the question of how small a non-vanishing sum of $N$-th roots of unity with $k$ terms can be. We show upper bounds of the shape $N^{-α_k}$, where $α_k\rightarrow\infty$ with $k$. We also address the question of improving these bounds for a positive proportion of $N$.

Large Sets of Integers with No Harmonic Triples

Samuel Korsky

How large can a set of integers be if no three elements form a 'harmonic triple' — meaning 2/a = 1/b + 1/c? This paper gives a new lower bound for this maximum size, showing such sets can be surprisingly large. The construction cleverly combines randomness with structure borrowed from arithmetic progressions in finite fields, advancing our understanding of additive combinatorics problems analogous to the famous cap-set and Behrend-type problems.

math.NT math.CO
Original abstract

Let $f(N)$ denote the largest size of a set $A\subseteq [N]=\{1,\ldots,N\}$ containing no distinct $a,b,c$ such that \[ \frac2a=\frac1b+\frac1c . \] We prove \[ f(N)\gg N\exp\!\left(-(2\sqrt{\log(24/7)}+o(1))\sqrt{\log\log N}\right). \] The construction filters the odd integers up to $N$ by a random affine image of a dense three-term-progression-free set in a prime field $\mathbb{F}_q$ with $q\asymp\log N$, and then deletes a controlled family of collapsed triples.

The Euler Ensemble as a Turbulent Attractor: Parity Sectors, Zero Modes, and a Zeta Edge

Alexander Migdal

This paper analyzes the stability of special exact solutions — called Euler ensembles — of equations governing turbulent fluid flow, by computing their Lyapunov spectrum (rates measuring how nearby solutions diverge). A key surprise: stability depends only on a single winding number summarizing a sequence of ±1 choices, reducing a complex problem to number theory involving the Riemann zeta function. This connects turbulence mathematics to deep arithmetic structure.

nlin.CD math.NT physics.flu-dyn
Original abstract

We compute the Lyapunov spectrum of the finite Euler ensembles, compact arithmetic fixed points of the rescaled momentum-loop equation for freely decaying incompressible Navier--Stokes turbulence. At finite cutoff \(N\), the tangential linearized problem is exactly solvable: the full Ising history \(σ_k=\pm1\) enters only through the closure winding \(qr=\sum_{k=1}^Nσ_k\). The stability problem therefore reduces to an arithmetic spectral problem over reduced rational angles \(p/q\) and winding sectors \(r\). The continuum limit splits into three local sectors. For odd \(N\), both \(q\) and \(r\) are odd, so \(r=0\) is excluded by parity. For even \(N\), the zero-winding sector \(r=0\) is allowed and must be separated from the punctured sector \(r\ne0\). Their partition functions satisfy \(Z_{e,0}(N)/Z_{e,*}(N)\sim 6N/π^2\), so the zero-winding sector is a singular discrete zero mode, not part of the Gaussian \(r\)-continuum. The even zero-winding ensemble has a continuous tangential spectrum with positive Lyapunov exponents and is unstable. In the odd and punctured even ensembles, the spectral angle remains quantized, and for every fixed spectral label \(n\) the normalized eigenvalue law converges weakly to \(δ_0\). Thus these two sectors are marginal fixed-mode Lyapunov limits. Their finite positive eigenvalues survive only as a vanishing arithmetic edge governed by coprime cotangent sums, Jordan totients, Dirichlet convolution, and \(ζ(s)\). For \(d>2\), transverse perturbations are zero modes at linear order; in the two marginal sectors their quadratic obstruction is absorbed by a radial correction, leaving no quadratic spectral shift.

Integer values of $\tan(\arctan 1+\arctan 2+\cdots+\arctan n)$ are rare

Ken Ono

Is the tangent of the sum arctan(1)+arctan(2)+…+arctan(n) ever an integer? A 2008 conjecture says no for n≥5, but it was only verified for about 15% of integers. This paper proves that any integer value would have to be astronomically large — at least e^(n log n / 2) — implying the conjecture holds for 100% of n in a density sense, with at most O(log N) exceptions up to N.

math.NT math.CO
Original abstract

For $n\ge1$, we let $$x_n:=\tan\bigl(\sum_{k=1}^{n}\arctan k\bigr).$$ In 2008, Amdeberhan, Medina, and Moll conjectured that $x_n\not \in \mathbb{Z}$ for every $n\ge5$. This was known for a set of positive integers of density $\tfrac{120}{817}\approx0.1469$. We prove that an integer value $x_n=m$ satisfies $|m|\ge e^{(1/2+o(1))\,n\log n}$, which we use to deduce that $$\#\{\,1\leq n\le N:x_n\in\mathbb{Z}\,\}=O(\log N). $$ In particular, the conjecture holds for a density-one set of $n$. The results in this note were formalized in Lean/Mathlib and produced autonomously by AxiomProver from natural-language statements.

Sharp Circular Sampling and Derivative Period Polynomials

Seokho Jin

This paper asks: if a function's values on the unit circle satisfy certain symmetry conditions, when are all zeros of a related polynomial forced onto the unit circle? It identifies a sharp hyperbolic region Ω_d of allowed 'reflected zeros' that guarantees this, and proves no larger region works. The result refines connections between the Riemann Hypothesis program, de Bruijn–Newman theory, and the zero distribution of period polynomials attached to modular forms.

math.NT
Original abstract

We determine the exact maximal reflected zero region that forces centered binomial samples of a balanced entire function to have all zeros on the unit circle. In degree $d\ge2$, this region is \[ Ω_d=\left\{a+ib:\ a^2-\frac{b^2}{d-1}\le\frac d4\right\}. \] The finite theorem is sharp already for a single reflected zero pair, and a phase-preserving canonical-product approximation extends it to balanced entire functions of order at most one. De Bruijn strip contraction and projective Hermite--Kakeya--Obreschkoff theory then give the exact common-zero obstruction, simplicity, strict interlacing of consecutive derivative samples, and a monotone real-pencil root flow. As an application, we prove the derivative-period-polynomial unit-circle theorem for completed $L$-functions of primitive holomorphic newforms, in every derivative order and for arbitrary level and nebentypus. After the standard normalization, every zero of \[ \sum_{j=0}^{k-2}\binom{k-2}{j}Λ^{(m)}(f,j+1)z^j \] lies on the unit circle for every weight $k\ge4$, level, nebentypus, and derivative order $m\ge0$. In particular, this proves the full-polynomial unit-circle conjecture of Diamantis and Rolen in its original level-one setting and extends it to arbitrary level and nebentypus. The same source-side theorem also gives simplicity, strict interlacing, and, for each fixed derivative order, conductor-uniform quantitative localization in the weight aspect.

Thue equations that simultaneously fail the Hasse principle

Paloma Bengoechea

A Thue equation is a Diophantine equation F(x,y)=h where F is a high-degree binary form; the 'Hasse principle' asks whether having solutions modulo every integer implies a genuine integer solution. This paper shows that a positive proportion of Thue equations simultaneously fail to have integer solutions for every right-hand side h up to any fixed bound m, even when local solutions exist everywhere—dramatically strengthening previous counterexamples to the Hasse principle.

math.NT
Original abstract

We refine a previous construction by Akhtari and Bhargava so that, for every positive integer m, we obtain a positive proportion of Thue equations F(x; y) = h that fail the integral Hasse principle simultaneously for every positive integer h less than m. The binary forms F have fixed degree >2 and are ordered by the absolute value of the maximum of the coefficients.

Exceptional rational functions of degree 5 over finite fields: classification by monodromy, ramification, and the Riemann-Hurwitz formula

Zhichao Tang, Xiang Fan

An 'exceptional' rational function over a finite field is one that acts as a bijection on infinitely many field extensions—a rare and useful property. This paper completes the full classification of such functions of degree 5 (outside characteristics 2 and 5), using monodromy groups (which encode how roots permute under loops) and the Riemann-Hurwitz formula to systematically eliminate cases. This closes a long-standing gap in the classification program for exceptional polynomials.

math.NT
Original abstract

We complete the classification of exceptional rational functions of degree 5 over any finite field $\mathbb{F}_{q}$ of characteristic different from 2 and 5, up to Möbius equivalence. Our approach employs the arithmetic and geometric monodromy groups, combined with the ramification structure of the associated cover and the Riemann-Hurwitz formula. The cyclic monodromy case yields precisely monomials and Rédei functions, while the dihedral case is resolved by analysing its inertia groups and branch points; this leads to Dickson polynomials and a non-polynomial family arising from rational 5-isogenies of elliptic curves. We also obtain partial classifications in characteristics 2 and 5: all cases with cyclic geometric monodromy and all dihedral cases admitting an $\mathbb{F}_{q}$-rational branch point are determined. The only unresolved cases are those with geometric monodromy group $D_{5}$ and no $\mathbb{F}_{q}$-rational branch point.

Pythagorean triples in level sets of completely multiplicative functions

Guilherme Azevedo, Joel Moreira

Can every finite coloring of the natural numbers be avoided for Pythagorean triples? This paper proves a special case: for any completely multiplicative functions (functions satisfying f(mn)=f(m)f(n)) mapping integers to the unit circle, there are Pythagorean triples x²+y²=z² where all functions simultaneously take values near 1, combining tools from analytic number theory around 'pretentious' and 'aperiodic' multiplicative functions.

math.NT math.CO
Original abstract

We show that given completely multiplicative functions $f_1,\dots,f_d$ taking values in the unit circle, there exist Pythagorean triples (i.e., integer solutions to $x^2+y^2=z^2$) with $f_i(x),f_i(y),f_i(z)$ all arbitrarily close to $1$ for all $i$. This is a new special case of the conjecture that any finite colouring of $\mathbb{N}$ has a monochromatic Pythagorean triple. Our proof combines vanishing averages for aperiodic functions with concentration estimates for pretentious functions. A similar proof is applied to obtain the analogous statement for more general equations of the form $ax^2+by^2=cz^2$ whenever $a,b,c$ are perfect squares satisfying the Rado's condition.

A decades-long breakthrough in zero-density estimates and primes in short intervals

Caroline L. Turnage-Butterbaugh

The Riemann Hypothesis predicts where the Riemann zeta-function's zeros live; 'zero-density theorems' bound how many zeros can stray from that predicted location. Guth and Maynard's 2024 breakthrough — the first improvement in over 80 years — sharpened these bounds and yielded new results about primes in short intervals. This expository paper explains the mathematical context and significance of that landmark advance.

math.NT
Original abstract

The Riemann Hypothesis (RH) asserts that every nontrivial zero of the Riemann zeta-function has real part equal to $1/2$. A zero-density theorem provides evidence towards RH by bounding the number of zeros of the zeta-function with real part greater than $1/2$. In 2024, Larry Guth and James Maynard announced a new zero-density theorem which, for a key location in the critical strip, strengthens previous work of Ingham and is the first such improvement in over 80 years. This expository paper places this remarkable achievement in the context of the rich history of zero-density theorems and explores its implications on the distribution of primes in short intervals.

Supercongruences Arising from Truncated Appell Series

He-Xia Ni

Appell series are two-variable generalizations of classical hypergeometric series, and 'supercongruences' are surprising divisibility properties where truncated series agree with mathematical constants modulo high prime powers. Using the 'creative microscoping' method, this paper establishes q-analogues — deformations depending on a parameter q that recover classical results as q→1 — of known supercongruences, including new cases for truncated Appell F₁ and F₂ series.

math.NT
Original abstract

In recent years, both Appell series and truncated Appell series have garnered significant research interest among scholars. In this paper, we investigate the congruence properties of truncated Appell series and truncated $q$-Appell series. By employing various combinatorial identities and the `creative microscoping' method introduced by Guo and Zudilin, we provide $q$-analogues for several known congruences of truncated Appell series $F_1$ and $F_2$. Meanwhile, we establish a new $q$-supercongruence related to the truncated Appell series $F_4$ and propose a related conjecture, which provides a $q$-analogue for two conjectures by Apagodu and Zeilberger.

Symmetric Spectral Reciprocity for $\mathrm{GL}(2)$ and Uniform Subconvexity

Liyang Yang

How well can we bound L-functions—complex-analytic objects encoding deep arithmetic information—away from their trivial estimates? This paper derives a new symmetric spectral reciprocity formula for fourth moments of GL(2) L-functions over number fields, then uses it to achieve uniform subconvexity bounds. Subconvexity (beating the 'convexity barrier') is a major goal in analytic number theory with consequences for equidistribution and prime distribution problems.

math.NT
Original abstract

We construct a new analytic regularization of the Petersson norm identity for Eisenstein series on $\mathrm{GL}_2$ over a number field $F$, and derive from it an explicit symmetric spectral reciprocity formula for twisted fourth moments of $\mathrm{GL}_2$ $L$-functions, reflecting the intrinsic rank decomposition $4=2+2$. Independently, we identify a square-level phenomenon arising from amplification, whereby the dual spectral family acquires square-level conductors. This additional arithmetic rigidity permits a refined analysis within the relative trace formula and leads to refined hybrid subconvexity bounds for twisted $L$-functions. As a consequence, we obtain new uniform subconvexity bounds for $\mathrm{GL}_2/F$; in particular, \begin{align*} L(1/2,π)\ll C(π)^{\frac14-\frac{1}{120}+\varepsilon} \end{align*} for every unitary cuspidal representation $π$ of $\mathrm{GL}_2/F$. We also obtain refined bounds for certain Artin $L$-functions and applications to class group arithmetic.

Improving on the Brun-Titchmarsh Theorem

Olvier Ramaré, Jan Christoph Schlage-Puchta

How many primes can fit in an interval of length N? The classical Brun–Titchmarsh theorem bounds this by roughly 2N/log N, but this paper sharpens the denominator to log N + 3.53 for large N. The improvement comes from a hybrid sieve combining the large sieve and Selberg sieve with novel 'local models' capturing prime distribution more precisely. Tighter prime-counting bounds have downstream applications throughout analytic number theory.

math.NT
Original abstract

We prove that the number of primes in an interval of length $N$ is at most $2N/(\log N+3.53)$ when $N$ is large enough. This is obtained through a sieving process which can be seen as a hybrid between the large sieve and the Selberg sieve, and draws on what we call "local models". This paper has been published in {\sc Acta Arithmetica, 2008, vol. 131, nb. 4, pages 351--366}.

On the number of perfect positive definite quadratic forms

Nikolay Moshchevitin

How many 'perfect' quadratic forms exist in n variables? A quadratic form is perfect if it's uniquely determined by its shortest vectors, and counting them is a hard open problem. This paper proves a new asymptotic lower bound approaching the conjectured optimal growth rate, significantly advancing our understanding of how these arithmetically important objects proliferate with dimension.

math.NT
Original abstract

We prove a new asymptotic lower bound for the number of perfect positive definite quadratic forms in n variables which is close to the optimal one.

Distinctness of the generalized $n$-dimensional Kloosterman sums

Xin Lin

Kloosterman sums are exponential sums over finite fields central to number theory and cryptography. This paper uses Galois theory and the Stickelberger theorem—a classical result describing prime factorizations of Gauss sums—to determine when generalized n-dimensional Kloosterman sums over a finite field are algebraically distinct. The results shed light on the arithmetic structure underlying these fundamental objects.

math.NT
Original abstract

In this paper, we study the algebraic degree and distinctness of a kind of generalized $n$-dimensional Kloosterman sums over finite field $F_q$ by Galois theory and the Stickelberger theorem on $p$-adic Gauss sums.

A Stretched-Exponential Bound for an Erdos--Graham Unit-Fraction Problem

Samuel Korsky

Erdős and Graham asked whether, given a set of positive integers with reciprocal sum exceeding K, you can always find a subset of reciprocals summing within exp(-cK) of 1. This paper proves a stretched-exponential bound of exp(-c√(K log K)), the strongest result to date toward their conjecture. Unit-fraction problems like this sit at the intersection of additive combinatorics and elementary number theory, and closing the gap remains open.

math.NT math.CO
Original abstract

For a finite multiset $A$ of positive integers, write $\mathcal{R}(A)=\sum_{a\in A}a^{-1}$ and let $\varepsilon(A)$ be the distance from $1$ to the largest reciprocal subsum of $A$ that does not exceed $1$. Erdős and Graham proved that $\varepsilon(A)\ll K^{-2}$ whenever $\mathcal{R}(A)>K$, and asked whether one always has $\varepsilon(A)\leq \exp(-cK)$ for an absolute constant $c>0$. We prove the stretched-exponential estimate $$ \varepsilon(A)\leq \exp\bigl(-c\sqrt{K\log K}\bigr) $$ for all sufficiently large $K$.

$p$-adic multiple $L$-functions and twisted multiple Bernoulli numbers

Ku-Yu Fan

p-adic L-functions are number-theoretic objects that interpolate classical L-functions using p-adic arithmetic, and their special values at integers encode deep algebraic information. This paper gives explicit formulas for these special values using twisted multiple Bernoulli numbers—generalizations of classical Bernoulli numbers—making previously abstract expressions concretely computable. The work advances our ability to calculate with p-adic multiple L-functions, objects central to modern algebraic number theory.

math.NT
Original abstract

We compute the special values ($p$MLFVs) of the $p$-adic multiple $L$-functions introduced by Furusho, Komori, Matsumoto, and Tsumura at tuples of positive integers. Furusho and Jarossay show that the special values can be expressed as an infinite sum of cyclotomic multiple harmonic values (CMHVs) with coefficients given by cyclotomic multiple Bernoulli numbers (CMBNs). We provide an explicit formula for CMBNs in terms of twisted multiple Bernoulli numbers (TMBNs), which are special values of generalized Euler-Zagier-Lerch type complex multiple zeta functions at tuples of non-positive integers. As a result, we obtain that these $p$MLFVs can be expressed as infinite sums of CMHVs, with coefficients given by the special values of the complex functions at tuples of non-positive integers.

Extreme values of derivatives of Dirichlet $L$-functions

Xinghua Cui, Zhifeng Peng, Yutong Song et al.

Dirichlet L-functions are complex-analytic functions encoding prime distribution in arithmetic progressions, and their extreme values in the critical strip (½ < σ < 1) are poorly understood. This paper shows that derivatives of these L-functions can be just as large as the functions themselves in that range—matching lower bounds previously known only for the original L-functions. This surprising result suggests derivatives don't smooth out the wild oscillations that make L-functions analytically difficult.

math.NT
Original abstract

In this paper, we establish lower bounds for extreme values of derivatives of Dirichlet \(L\)-functions in the range \(1/2<σ<1\). Compared with the work of Aistleitner, Mahatab, Munsch, and Peyrot in 2019, our result shows that derivatives of Dirichlet \(L\)-functions can attain extreme values of the same order of magnitude as the original \(L\)-functions when \(1/2<σ<1\) holds.

Anomalous Partial Quotients in the Continued Fraction of $\sqrt{ζ(3)-S_N}$

David Victor Feldman

The continued fraction of a number expresses it as a sequence of integer 'partial quotients,' which are typically small for structured constants. This paper proves that the square root of the tail of Apéry's constant ζ(3) has anomalously large partial quotients—growing cubically rather than linearly—at indices tied to Pell numbers, an integer sequence satisfying Pell's equation x²−2y²=1. The result reveals unexpected arithmetic structure hiding in the approximation theory of a famous constant.

math.NT
Original abstract

Let $S_N = \sum_{j=1}^N j^{-3}$ and $R_N = ζ(3) - S_N$. The simple continued fraction of $\sqrt{R_N}$ has partial quotients of generic size $O(N)$. We prove that at the sequence of indices $N_k = (Q_{2k+1}-1)/2$, where $Q_{2k+1}$ are companion Pell numbers, the continued fraction begins \[ \sqrt{R_{N_k}} = \bigl[0;\; M_k-1,\; 1,\; 6M_k^3+12M_k-2,\; 1,\; \ldots\,\bigr], \] with $M_k = P_{2k+1}$ (Pell numbers), and the third partial quotient grows cubically while generic ones are linear. We determine all partial quotients through the fifth: \begin{align*} \PQ_0 &= M_k - 1, & \PQ_2 &= 6M_k^3 + 12M_k - 2, & \PQ_4 &= \Bigl\lfloor\frac{10M_k - 261}{261}\Bigr\rfloor, \PQ_1 &= 1, & \PQ_3 &= 1, & \PQ_5 &= \Bigl\lfloor\frac{261}{r_k}\Bigr\rfloor + ε_k, \end{align*} where $r_k = (10M_k) \bmod 261$ satisfies the recurrence $r_{k+1} \equiv 6r_k - r_{k-1} \pmod{261}$, and $ε_k = -1$ at the $k$ with $r_k \mid 261$ (the two residue classes $k \equiv 57, 62 \pmod{60}$), and $ε_k = 0$ otherwise. All six formulas follow from the Euler--Maclaurin expansion of $1/\sqrt{R_{N_k}}$, carried to sufficient precision, combined with the Pell identity $Q_{2k+1}^2 - 2M_k^2 = -1$. The delicate first step, $\PQ_0 = M_k - 1$, is proved by rationalizing the irrational factor $\sqrt{2}$ in the Euler--Maclaurin expansion; we complement this proof with a heuristic derivation via Gosper's bihomographic continued-fraction algorithm that exposes the underlying mechanism. All claimed results have been formalized in LEAN with the aid of Aristotle.

Algebraic Geometry

27

The hyper-Kummer construction

Salvatore Floccari, Lie Fu

The classical Kummer construction turns an abelian surface into a K3 surface by quotienting by an involution and resolving singularities; this paper develops a higher-dimensional analogue. Starting from a six-dimensional hyper-Kähler manifold of generalized Kummer type, the hyper-Kummer construction produces an eight-dimensional hyper-Kähler manifold, and the authors prove characterization and lattice-theoretic results mirroring the classical theory.

math.AG
Original abstract

The hyper-Kummer construction, discovered by the first-named author, associates a hyper-Kähler manifold of $\mathrm{K}3^{[3]}$-type with any hyper-Kähler sixfold of generalized Kummer type. We regard this construction as a higher-dimensional analog of the classical Kummer construction of K3 surfaces from abelian surfaces. In this spirit, we prove several results which parallel those known for the classical Kummer construction: we characterize the hyper-Kummer $\mathrm{K}3^{[3]}$-manifolds up to birational equivalence in terms of their Hodge lattices, and establish a McKay correspondence for their derived categories and Chow motives. We propose a recipe to construct locally complete families of projective varieties of $\mathrm{Kum}^3$-type starting from a family of varieties of $\mathrm{K}3^{[3]}$-type equipped with 16 prime divisors in a certain Kummer lattice configuration. We also compare hyper-Kummer $\mathrm{K}3^{[3]}$-manifolds with the Mongardi-Rapagnetta-Saccà double covers of O'Grady's six-dimensional hyper-Kähler manifolds. The hyper-Kummer construction produces a rich configuration of hyper-Kähler manifolds of $\mathrm{K}3^{[2]}$-type and K3 surfaces canonically associated with a manifold of $\mathrm{Kum}^3$-type, in particular the hyper-Kummer K3 surfaces, which form countably many $4$-dimensional families of generic Picard rank 16. We prove abelianity of Chow motives for infinitely many 4-dimensional families of hyper-Kummer K3 surfaces, thereby proving Kimura-O'Sullivan finite-dimensionality conjecture for many new K3 surfaces of Picard rank 16. As other applications, we prove Beauville's weak splitting conjecture for all varieties of $\mathrm{Kum}^3$-type, and, building on previous results, we prove the Hodge and Tate conjectures for all powers of any of the varieties involved in the hyper-Kummer construction.

The Knop-Luna-Vust theory of spherical embeddings, extended to non-reductive groups

Guido Pezzini

Spherical embeddings classify how a reductive group acts on a variety with an open dense orbit, with the combinatorics neatly captured by convex cones. This paper extends that entire framework to non-reductive groups, maintaining the same cone-based description while uncovering new connections to the unipotent radical. As an application, the authors characterize log homogeneous varieties in this broader setting.

math.AG math.RT
Original abstract

We extend the theory of spherical embeddings to actions of connected non-reductive groups. This generalization is formally very similar to the usual reductive case: equivariant embeddings are described essentially by collections of convex cones in a rational vector space. We also show some new relationship between the combinatorics emerging in this case and the properties of the unipotent radical of the acting group. Finally, we apply our techniques to prove a characterization of log homogeneous varieties.

Involution-equivariant topological recursion and mirror symmetry for the affine binary dihedral Calabi--Yau threefold

Bohan Fang, Zhuoming Lan, Jingxiang Ma

Topological recursion is a recursive procedure that computes invariants of curves and, conjecturally, counts curves inside Calabi–Yau threefolds (special complex 3D spaces central to string theory). This paper proves the remodeling conjecture—linking open- and closed-string geometry via topological recursion—for a non-toric orbifold built from a binary dihedral symmetry group, a case previously out of reach. It extends mirror symmetry calculations beyond the well-understood toric setting by introducing an involution-equivariant version of the recursion.

math.AG math-ph
Original abstract

We prove a closed-string remodeling statement for the affine binary dihedral Calabi--Yau orbifold threefold $\mathcal X=[\mathbb C^2/Γ\times\mathbb C]$, where $Γ$ is a binary dihedral subgroup of $SU(2)$. This target lies outside the toric setting of the Bouchard--Klemm--Mariño--Pasquetti remodeling conjecture: the toric mirror curve is replaced by the type-$D_l$ logarithmic Toda curve of Brini--Ma--Strachan, and the Chekhov--Eynard--Orantin topological recursion is replaced by the $\mathbb Z_2$-equivariant topological recursion of Giacchetto--Kramer--Lewański, run in the sign sector of the Toda-curve involution with the Prym kernel as its two-point input. We identify the equivariant orbifold quantum cohomology Frobenius manifold of $\mathcal X$ with the invariant Jacobian Frobenius structure of the Toda curve, and we prove that the B-model $R$-matrix, defined by regularized stationary phase, equals the A-side normalized canonical Givental--Teleman $R$-matrix on the smooth oscillatory chamber; this equality is anchored at the orbifold point through a semistable degeneration of the Toda curve. Comparing the resulting Givental--Teleman and Dunin-Barkowski--Orantin--Shadrin--Spitz graph sums then identifies, after a parity-twisted leaf substitution, the sign-sector recursion with the descendant Gromov--Witten generating functions of $\mathcal X$ in the stable range ($2g-2+n>0$ with $n>0$), and identifies the recursion free energies with the equivariant Gromov--Witten free energies of $\mathcal X$ for $g\geq2$.

Descendability and descent in topological weaves

Adeel A. Khan

math.AG
Original abstract

We prove a criterion for a finitely presented surjection of algebraic spaces to be descendable in a topological weave. We apply this to show that rational motivic sheaves satisfy v-descent, and the same for étale motivic spectra on noetherian finite-dimensional schemes with residue fields of uniformly bounded étale cohomological dimension. We also construct the "forgetting supports" isomorphism $f_! \simeq f_*$ for a proper DM morphism of Artin stacks, in rational motivic sheaves.

On the Chow ring of very general abelian varieties and a question of Pirola

Claire Voisin

In the Chow ring of an abelian variety—an algebraic bookkeeping system for geometric cycles up to rational equivalence—when can a divisor D satisfy D²=0 without being a torsion element? This paper proves that for very general abelian varieties of dimension ≥4, such divisors must be torsion, and uses this to resolve Pirola's conjecture: any rational section of the Kummer fibration over the moduli space of genus-4 curves must be a multiple of the identity section.

math.AG
Original abstract

We prove that for a very general abelian variety of dimension $\geq 4$, a divisor $D\in {\rm CH}^1(A)$ that satisfies $D^2=0$ in ${\rm CH}^2(A)$ is of torsion. The same result is also established for a very general Jacobian in genus $4$. We use then the second statement in order to prove a conjecture of Pirola, which states that any rational section of the Kummer fibration $K=J/\pm {\rm Id}\rightarrow \mathcal{M}_4$, where $J\rightarrow \mathcal{M}_4 $ is the Jacobian fibration, must be a multiple of the Griffiths-Pirola section given by the difference of the two trigonal divisors.

Arithmetic of Gysin kernels

Vladimir Guletskii, Bo Zhang

When a curve sits inside a surface, it induces a natural map called the Gysin homomorphism between algebraic objects tracking 'cycles' on each. This paper studies the kernel of this map — the elements that vanish under it — using tools from arithmetic geometry, asking which algebraic cycles on the surface become trivial when restricted along a generic fiber curve. The results shed light on deep structural questions about Chow groups, which measure algebraic equivalence classes of subvarieties.

math.AG
Original abstract

Let $k$ be a field, and let $X$ be a smooth projective surface over $k$. Fix a Lefschetz pencil on $X$, and let $C$ be its fibre at the generic point of $\mathbb P^1$. The closed immersion of $C$ in to $X_{k(t)}$ induces the Gysin homomorphism from the Jacobian $A$ of the curve $C$ to the Chow group $A_0(X_{k(t)})$ of $0$-cycles of degree $0$ on $X_{k(t)}$. Embedding $k(t)$ in to an uncountable universal domain $\Bbbk $, we obtain the corresponding homomorphism from $A(\Bbbk )$ to $A_0(X_{\Bbbk })$, whose kernel is either countable or the union of translates of a certain abelian subvariety inside $A_{\Bbbk }$, due to the Deligne-Katz irreducibility of monodromy action on vanishing cycles. We prove three dichotomy theorems on the structure of the kernel of Gysin homomorphism on $0$-cycles, in terms of étale monodromy action, and working over a field of arbitrary characteristic.

The Algebraic Montgomery-Yang Problem

Woohyeok Jo, Jongil Park, Kyungbae Park

The Montgomery-Yang problem asks: can a rational homology projective plane—a surface resembling the complex projective plane in homology—with isolated quotient singularities have more than three singular points? This paper completely resolves Kollár's conjecture that the answer is no, using a clever combination of Donaldson's diagonalization theorem from gauge theory with lattice-theoretic constraints from the canonical spin^c structure. The result closes a long-standing question at the intersection of topology and algebraic geometry.

math.AG math.GT
Original abstract

We completely resolve the algebraic Montgomery - Yang problem, a conjecture of Kollar stating that every rational homology projective plane with quotient singularities and a simply-connected smooth locus has at most three singular points. The crux of our proof is a new lattice theoretic constraint, obtained by combining Donaldson's diagonalization theorem with the distinguished spin^c structure on the smooth locus whose determinant line bundle is the canonical bundle. Together with the orbifold Bogomolov - Miyaoka - Yau inequality, this constraint rules out all remaining cases in the problem and completes the proof.

Classification of equivariantly normal curves via Altmann-Hausen-Süss theory

Gary Martínez-Núnez, Ronan Terpereau

Over perfect fields of positive characteristic, this paper uses Altmann-Hausen-Süss theory—a combinatorial framework for algebraic varieties with group actions—to classify 'equivariantly normal curves,' curves carrying a group action compatible with their embedding. The classification is fully combinatorial and yields an explicit bound on the number of isomorphism classes for curves with prescribed branching behavior, making a previously intractable classification problem concrete and computable.

math.AG
Original abstract

Let the ground field be perfect of positive characteristic. Using Altmann-Hausen-Süss theory, we obtain a combinatorial classification of equivariantly normal curves with prescribed quotient in both the affine and projective settings. As a consequence, we derive an explicit upper bound on the number of isomorphism classes of equivariantly normal curves over a fixed base curve with prescribed branch locus. Furthermore, assuming that the ground field is algebraically closed, we determine, for a fixed cardinality of the branch locus, precisely when the set of isomorphism classes of equivariantly normal projective curves with prescribed quotient is finite and when it is infinite.

A generalised cross-ratio and limits of local heights

Hidde Lammert Bakker, Emre Can Sertöz

The classical cross-ratio measures how four points on a line relate projectively—a fundamental invariant in geometry. This paper generalizes it to configurations of four planes of complementary dimensions in projective n-space, then shows this invariant equals the augmented height pairing of corresponding algebraic cycles over the complex numbers. Over fields with a discrete valuation (like p-adic fields), the result describes how local heights degenerate, connecting classical projective geometry to modern arithmetic geometry.

math.AG math.NT
Original abstract

We generalise the standard cross-ratio of four points on a projective line to a cross-ratio of a configuration of four planes in projective $n$-space, the first pair $A_1,\,A_2$ being $k$-dimensional and the second pair $B_1,\,B_2$ being $(n-k-1)$-dimensional, with $A_i \cap B_j = \emptyset$. Over the complex numbers, we show that this cross-ratio equals the augmented height pairing of the corresponding cycles $A_1-A_2, \, B_1-B_2$. Over a discretely valued field, we show that the valuation of the cross-ratio equals the intersection degree of the cycles once they are spread out over the valuation ring. Putting the two together, we conclude that the asymptotics of the Archimedean height pairing of a holomorphic family of configurations are governed by this intersection degree. We also define a degenerate cross-ratio for when $A_i \cap B_j \neq \emptyset$ and interpret the "limit height" of a degenerating holomorphic family of planes as the degenerate cross-ratio of the central plane configuration.

Genus one correspondence between tropical and algebraic curves

Alessio Cela, Sae Koyama

Tropical geometry replaces algebraic curves with piecewise-linear combinatorial objects, and a 'correspondence theorem' says the count of algebraic curves equals the count of tropical ones. The celebrated Nishinou-Siebert theorem established this for genus-0 curves; this paper extends it to genus-1 (elliptic) curves in any toric variety, completing a long-sought generalization. The proof uses logarithmic deformation theory—a sophisticated tool for tracking how curves degenerate—to handle the extra complexity that loops introduce.

math.AG
Original abstract

We show that the genuinely enumerative count of algebraic elliptic curves in any toric variety agrees with the count of the corresponding well-spaced tropical curves, weighted by explicit combinatorial multiplicities. This provides a complete genus-$1$ generalization of the celebrated Nishinou--Siebert correspondence theorem in genus $0$. The proof is algebro-geometric and relies on logarithmic deformation theory together with an explicit enumeration of logarithmic maps with fixed tropicalization.

Microlocal Bernstein--Sato polynomials on singular ambient varieties

Bradley Dirks, Michael Perlman

The Bernstein–Sato polynomial is an invariant encoding singularity data of a function, classically defined on smooth spaces. This paper extends the theory to singular ambient varieties, showing that key results from the smooth setting—like a theorem of Saito linking these polynomials to Hodge theory—break down or require careful reformulation. The work provides new tools for studying singularities of divisors and their Hodge filtrations in the more general, geometrically realistic singular setting.

math.AG
Original abstract

We introduce the microlocal Bernstein--Sato polynomial of a function on a possibly singular ambient variety, extending the theory of Saito. We show that, contrary to the smooth ambient setting, these polynomials are not generally equal to the reduced $b$-functions obtained by removing the trivial root. We define the minimal exponent and use it to study the singularities of the divisor and the Hodge filtration on local cohomology. Our main results include a generalization of Saito's theorem relating the minimal exponent to rational singularities, a characterization of purity of local cohomology, a Thom--Sebastiani formula for the minimal exponent, and a linear combination formula for Bernstein--Sato polynomials of ideals. When the ambient variety is a complete intersection with rational singularities, we provide effective algorithms for these Bernstein--Sato polynomials and implement them in Macaulay2.

Motivic quasimap wall-crossing for Grassmannians

Siddarth Kannan, Terry Dekun Song

Quasimaps are generalizations of maps from curves into a target space like a Grassmannian, and varying a stability parameter ε produces different moduli spaces related by 'wall-crossing.' This paper proves a wall-crossing formula at the level of motivic Euler characteristics—virtual mixed Hodge structures, which encode richer cohomological data than ordinary numbers—for Grassmannian targets. This lifts known numerical wall-crossing identities to a finer algebraic invariant, deepening the connection between enumerative geometry and representation theory.

math.AG math-ph math.CO
Original abstract

We prove a wall-crossing formula for the Euler characteristics, considered as virtual mixed Hodge structures, of moduli spaces of $\varepsilon$-stable quasimaps to the Grassmannian $\mathbb{G}(r, N)$. For each $\varepsilon > 0$, we define a $\mathbb{Q}$-algebra automorphism of the ring of symmetric functions which takes the generating function for the $\mathbb{S}_n$-equivariant Euler characteristics of the moduli spaces of stable maps $\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g, n}(\mathbb{G}(r, N), d)$ to the corresponding generating function for Toda's moduli spaces of $\varepsilon$-stable quasimaps $\overline{\mathcal{Q}}_{g, n}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbb{G}(r, N), d)$. The automorphism is given by explicit $q$-deformations of the power sum symmetric functions. The $\varepsilon \to 0$ limit of our formula exchanges the spaces of stable maps and the Marian--Oprea--Pandharipande moduli spaces of stable quotients. Our proof uses the geometry of relative Quot schemes to relate the quasimap spaces to moduli spaces of weighted stable maps, for which we obtain wall-crossing formulas via symmetric function theory.

Brauer groups of resolved quiver moduli via gerbes

Pieter Belmans, Gianni Petrella, Sebastián Torres

The Brauer group — an algebraic invariant measuring 'twisted' line bundles — of any smooth resolution of a certain singular geometric space turns out to be trivial. The paper proves this for moduli spaces (parameter spaces) of quiver representations, algebraic objects encoding directed graphs with vector spaces at vertices, by translating a known result from curve geometry. This gives a cleaner, unified proof of a result previously established through more computational means.

math.AG math.RT
Original abstract

We show that the Brauer group of any resolution of singularities of the moduli space of semistable quiver representations is trivial. We do this by extending the quiver-curve dictionary, translating a proof of the analogous result by Biswas-Hogadi-Holla for moduli of vector bundles on a curve to the setting of moduli of quiver representations, giving an algebro-geometric proof. This gives a new proof of this triviality, first proved by Le Bruyn-Schofield, building on algebraic (resp. cohomological) vanishing results due to Saltman (resp. Colliot-Thélène-Sansuc). Reversing the logic, our approach gives a new algebro-geometric proof of these vanishing results.

Singular fibers of elliptic fibrations on normal $K3$ surfaces

Taro Hayashi, Kazuki Utsumi

K3 surfaces are complex surfaces with rich geometric structure, and elliptic fibrations are maps from them onto a curve whose generic fiber is a torus. This paper classifies the singular (degenerate) fibers that can appear when the K3 surface itself has singularities, by systematically describing how contracting certain curve configurations in the smooth resolution transforms the known fiber types. The result extends classical fiber classification to the singular setting.

math.AG
Original abstract

We study singular fibers of elliptic fibrations on normal \(K3\) surfaces. For a normal \(K3\) surface \(Y\) with minimal resolution \(ν\colon X \to Y\), we describe singular fibers on \(Y\) in terms of contractions of suitable ADE configurations of \((-2)\)-curves in singular fibers on \(X\). We determine ADE configurations occurring in singular fibers and describe fibers obtained after contraction. As a consequence, we obtain a description of singular fibers of elliptic fibrations on normal \(K3\) surfaces.

Tangent classes of matroids and wonderful compactifications

Ronnie Cheng, Shurui Liu, Guoxiong Gao

A matroid is a combinatorial abstraction of linear independence; its wonderful compactification is a smooth algebraic variety encoding its structure. This paper constructs a purely combinatorial 'tangent class' for any loopless matroid that, in geometric cases, matches the actual tangent bundle, and recovers known intersection-theoretic data via Hirzebruch–Riemann–Roch. The result extends differential-geometric intuition into the combinatorial realm, strengthening the bridge between matroid theory and algebraic geometry.

math.AG cs.AI math.CO
Original abstract

For every loopless matroid $M$ and every Feichtner--Yuzvinsky building set $\mathcal{G}$ containing the top flat, we construct an integral tangent class $T_{M,\mathcal{G}}^{\mathbb{Z}}\in K_{\mathbb{Z}}(M,\mathcal{G})$; in the realizable case it specializes to the class of the tangent bundle of the corresponding wonderful compactification, it recovers the Hilbert series of the Chow ring through Hirzebruch--Riemann--Roch, and it satisfies the expected Chern-alpha lower bounds. This reproduces the tangent class and its key properties studied by the first author in arXiv:2606.22650. Danus solved the problem before arXiv:2606.22650 was publicly available, demonstrating the potential of AI agents in mathematical research. Algebraic Geometry of Electroid Varieties

Dawei Shen, Mia Smith, David E Speyer

Electroid varieties are geometric objects generalizing 'positroid varieties' (which stratify the Grassmannian, a space parameterizing linear subspaces) to a Lagrangian Grassmannian arising in electrical network theory. The authors investigate their algebraic geometry—including irreducibility, singularities, and cohomology—mirroring the rich combinatorial-geometric story Postnikov's theory provided for positroids, and extending the combinatorial groundwork Lam laid in 2018.

math.AG math.CO
Original abstract

Recent work of Lam, Bychkov-Gorbounov-Kazakov-Talalaev, and Chepuri-George-Speyer gave a stratification of the totally nonnegative Lagrangian Grassmannian into electroid cells parameterized by cactus networks, paralleling Postnikov's stratification of the totally nonnegative Grassmannian by positroid cells. Electroid varieties arise as an algebro-geometric extension of electroid cells. The combinatorics of these varieties was studied by Lam in 2018. We build on this work and study the geometric properties of electroid varieties. In analogy to results of Knutson, Lam, and Speyer on positroid varieties, we show that electroid varieties are reduced, irreducible, regular in codimension one, compatibly Frobenius split, and form a stratification. We also show a decomposition of certain electroid varieties as a product of two electroid varieties. As a consequence, the grove measurement map that embeds electroid cells can be extended algebraically to embed an algebraic torus.

Virtual Jeffrey--Kirwan localisation

Riccardo Ontani, Richard P. Thomas

How do you compute integrals over 'virtual cycles'—a generalized notion of fundamental class used in modern enumerative geometry—when your space has a group symmetry? This paper extends the classical Jeffrey–Kirwan localization formula to the virtual setting, expressing such integrals on GIT quotients (spaces built by quotienting by a group) in terms of data on fixed-point loci, covering both cohomological and K-theoretic settings and noncompact spaces.

math.AG math.SG
Original abstract

We express integrals over virtual cycles of GIT quotients $X/\!\!/G$ in terms of integrals over virtual cycles of fixed loci $X^T$. The results hold for both perfect obstruction theories and $(-2)$-shifted symplectic structures, in cohomology and in $K$-theory, and for noncompact Deligne-Mumford stacks acted on by a reductive group with compact quotient.

A note on stability conditions on projective spaces

Yiran Cheng

Bridgeland stability conditions are a sophisticated algebraic-geometry tool for organizing objects in derived categories—think of them as generalizing classical notions of which vector bundles are 'well-behaved.' This paper gives a new, streamlined proof that such conditions exist on projective spaces, and shows they can be restricted to produce stability conditions on any smooth projective variety.

math.AG
Original abstract

We give a new proof of Li's theorem on the existence of geometric Bridgeland stability conditions on the bounded derived category of coherent sheaves on projective spaces. These stability conditions can then be restricted to induce Bridgeland stability conditions on arbitrary smooth projective varieties.

A universal discriminant formula for pencils of quadrics

Ari Krishna

A pencil of quadrics is a one-parameter family of quadratic hypersurfaces in projective space; its discriminant tracks when the family becomes singular. This paper computes an explicit formula for the cohomology class of the locus of 'bad' pencils — those whose discriminant polynomial has a repeated root — inside the Grassmannian of all pencils. The result gives a universal, coordinate-free discriminant formula with applications to classical algebraic geometry and intersection theory.

math.AG
Original abstract

Let $V$ be a vector space of dimension $n+1$ over an algebraically closed field $\mathbb{k}$ of characteristic zero, and let $G_n = \operatorname{Gr}(2,\operatorname{Sym}^2V^\vee)$ be the Grassmannian parametrizing pencils of quadrics in $\mathbb{P}(V) \cong \mathbb{P}^n$. The determinant of the universal pencil defines a universal binary form of degree $n+1$. We prove that the divisor $\mathcal{D}_n\subseteq G_n$ of pencils whose determinant binary form has a multiple root has Chow class $[\mathcal{D}_n]=n(n+1)σ_1\in A^1(G_n),$ where $σ_1=c_1(S^\vee)$ and $S$ is the tautological rank-two subbundle on $G_n$. More generally, the higher-contact loci of determinant binary forms are computed by a universal jet formula. We also formulate the determinant-root collision strata as refined pullbacks of the universal collision strata for binary forms. For $n=3$, the main formula recovers the class $12σ_1$ for the boundary divisor in $\operatorname{Gr}(2,10)$ that the author established in a prior paper.

Toric decomposition in algebraic groups

Dávid R. Szabó

Can you decompose every element of a group as a product of elements from a small number of 'diagonal-like' subgroups? This paper shows that PGL_n—the group of invertible matrices modulo scaling—can be almost entirely covered by unique products from just n+1 maximal tori (maximal abelian subgroups of diagonalizable elements), and proves this count is optimal. The result establishes a clean structural decomposition theorem that works over any field.

math.AG math.GR
Original abstract

Over an arbitrary field $\mathbb{F}$, we construct $n+1$ maximal tori $T_1,\dots,T_{n+1}$ in $\operatorname{PGL}_n(\mathbb{F})$ so that the product $T_1\dots T_{n+1}$ is almost the whole $\operatorname{PGL}_n(\mathbb{F})$ and every $g\in T_1\dots T_{n+1}$ can be expressed uniquely as $g=t_1\dots t_{n+1}$ where $t_i\in T_i$. The construction is optimal, as the number of tori with this property attains a general upper bound for connected reductive groups over an algebraically closed field, as well as over finite fields. We also show that $n+2$ suitably chosen maximal tori $T_1,\dots,T_{n+2}$ are enough to cover the whole group, i.e. $\operatorname{PGL}_n(\mathbb{F})=T_1\dots T_{n+2}$, provided $|\mathbb{F}|>n^2$. This is optimal over a finite field and is conjecturally optimal over algebraically closed fields, i.e. the number of such tori is as small as possible.

A note on prismatic sites for p-quasisyntomic rings

Jingbang Guo

Prismatic cohomology is a powerful modern tool unifying various p-adic cohomology theories in arithmetic geometry, built on a category called the prismatic site. This note identifies special objects — called transversal and relatively quasiregular semiperfectoid covers — that serve as effective 'covering' objects in this site for quasisyntomic rings, clarifying the site's sheaf theory. This matters for computing prismatic cohomology in concrete cases beyond the perfectoid setting.

math.AG math.AC
Original abstract

Let $p$ be a fixed prime number and let $R$ be a $p$-quasisyntomic ring. In this note, we provide conditions for objects in the absolute prismatic site $R_\Prism$ to cover the final object in $\Shv(R_\Prism)$. More precisely, we introduce in $R_\Prism$ the so-called transversal objects, with which coproducts exist in $R_\Prism$. Immediately generalizing this, we introduce the so-called relatively quasiregular semiperfectoid covers of $R$, whose prismatic cohomology (of $δ$-pairs, in the sense of Antieau-Krause-Nikolaus) would produce in $R_\Prism$ objects with which coproducts exist.

A counterexample to the odd-dimensional rank bound for abelian \texorpdfstring{$p$}{p}-group actions

Jihao Liu, Yanze Wang

This paper disproves a conjectured bound on the rank of abelian p-group actions faithfully acting on odd-dimensional smooth Calabi-Yau varieties (complex manifolds with trivial canonical bundle). Notably, the authors disclose that the main mathematical result was produced by ChatGPT 5.5 pro, raising immediate questions about verification and the role of AI in mathematical research.

math.AG
Original abstract

We disprove the odd-dimensional extension, conjectured by Moraga and recorded by Kollár and Zhuang, of the rank bound for faithful abelian $p$-group actions on smooth Calabi--Yau varieties. The main result of this paper was obtained by ChatGPT 5.5 pro, and the Danus system based on the Rethlas system.

Localization and Affine Schemes over $\mathbb{F}_1$

Luqiao Xu

The "field with one element" F₁ is a half-mythical object that doesn't exist as a conventional field but whose geometry would unify combinatorics and number theory. This paper builds rigorous commutative algebra over F₁ using the Connes–Consani framework—where algebraic structure is encoded via symmetric sets from homotopy theory—and constructs a working theory of localization and affine schemes, key tools for doing geometry in this exotic setting.

math.AG math.AC
Original abstract

We develop the basic notions of commutative algebra and algebraic geometry over the field with one element $\mathbb{F}_1$, working within the Connes-Consani framework, which models $\mathbb{F}_1$-algebras as monoid objects in the category of $Γ$-sets. In this setting, $\mathbb{F}_1$-algebras generalize commutative rings by encoding the algebraic structure functorially, using machinery originating in homotopy theory. Our main contribution is a theory of localization for $\mathbb{F}_1$-algebras and the construction of prime spectrum $\Spec A$ for a commutative $\mathbb{F}_1$-algebra $A$. We then prove that $Γ(X, \mathcal{O}_X) = A$ for any absolute affine scheme $X=\Spec A$ and establish an anti-equivalence between the category of commutative $\mathbb{F}_1$-algebras and the category of absolute affine schemes.

Stability conditions on threefolds

Yiran Cheng, Soheyla Feyzbakhsh

Bridgeland stability conditions are a sophisticated tool in algebraic geometry for organizing geometric objects (sheaves) on a space into well-behaved families. This paper identifies which stability conditions on projective space restrict nicely to three-dimensional subvarieties, shows these coincide with a previously constructed class via the double-tilt method, and uses this to prove the weak Bayer–Macrì–Toda conjecture, a key step toward constructing stability conditions on general threefolds.

math.AG
Original abstract

We investigate a subspace of Bridgeland stability conditions on $\PP^n$ satisfying the so-called Li condition. These are the stability conditions whose restriction to a smooth projective subvariety $X \subset \PP^n$ is again a stability condition. We then show that, when $X$ is a threefold, the restricted stability conditions coincide with those obtained via the double-tilt construction introduced by Bayer-Macrì-Toda. As an application, we prove the weak BMT conjecture.

Classification of Smooth Minimal Kähler Fourfolds Without Effective Divisors and Surfaces

Pisya Vikash

If a compact Kähler fourfold (a four complex-dimensional geometric space with compatible metric and complex structure) has no hypersurfaces or codimension-two subvarieties, what must it look like? This paper proves its canonical bundle—a key invariant encoding curvature—must be torsion, forcing the manifold to be either a complex torus quotient or an irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifold. This completes a classification that previously required stronger geometric assumptions.

math.AG
Original abstract

We prove that if \(X\) is a compact Kähler fourfold with pseudo--effective canonical bundle and no subvarieties of codimension one or two, then \(K_X\) is a torsion line bundle. By the Beauville--Bogomolov decomposition theorem, it follows that \(X\) is either a quotient of a complex torus or an irreducible holomorphic symplectic manifold.

A Frobenius-periodic vector bundle on a curve in characteristic p is one that returns to itself after repeatedly applying the Frobenius map. Analysis of PDEs

31

A Short Proof of Optimal Regularity for minimizers of the Alt-Phillips Problem

Kunyi, Ma

The Alt-Phillips free boundary problem asks for functions minimizing a certain energy functional where a 'free boundary'—a surface you don't know in advance—separates regions with different behavior. Optimal regularity means showing minimizers are as smooth as theoretically possible up to that boundary. This paper delivers a concise, self-contained proof of this result for the parameter range γ∈(0,1), streamlining previously technical arguments via a clean dichotomy method.

math.AP
Original abstract

We give a self-contained short proof of optimal regularity for minimizers of the Alt-Phillips Free Boundary Problem for $γ\in (0, 1)$. We adopt a dichotomy argument that originates from [DS20].

Time-periodic vortices near translating symmetric dipole patches

Luca Franzoi, Taoufik Hmidi, Riccardo Montalto

The 2D Euler equations govern ideal fluid flow, and vortex patches—regions of constant vorticity—provide important exact-style solutions. This paper proves that a translating pair of opposite-sign vortex patches (a dipole) can bifurcate into genuinely time-periodic solutions when the vortices are far apart, using a perturbative analysis near an integrable system. The result rigorously establishes the existence of a new family of periodic fluid motions near a classical configuration.

math.AP math-ph
Original abstract

We prove the existence of time-periodic solutions of the two-dimensional incompressible Euler equations bifurcating from a translating vortex pair. The reference configuration consists of two symmetric vortex patches of equal strength and opposite sign traveling at constant speed. In a regime of large separation between the vortices, the dynamics may be viewed as a small perturbation of an integrable system. Working in a co-moving frame and using the contour dynamics formulation, we reduce the problem to a nonlinear transport equation for the vortex boundaries. The linearized operator exhibits degeneracies associated with symmetries and transport effects. By combining a Lyapunov-Schmidt reduction, Nash-Moser scheme, and spectral analysis with sharp asymptotic expansions of the eigenvalues in order to overcome the degeneracy, we construct families of non-rigid, time-periodic vortex patch solutions for a large Cantor set of parameters. The analysis reveals that translating dipoles possess a surprisingly rich nearby dynamics, far beyond the classical rigid paradigm usually associated with vortex patch motion. More generally, the approach developed in this work is flexible and robust, and is expected to extend to a broader class of nonlocal PDEs from Fluid Mechanics with degenerate behaviours.

Unconditional Uniqueness for the Energy-critical and Energy-supercritical Quadratic NLS

Shunlin Shen

The nonlinear Schrödinger equation can have multiple solutions from the same initial data unless you prove 'unconditional uniqueness' — that solutions are unique in the natural energy space without extra assumptions. This paper establishes such uniqueness for the quadratic NLS in high dimensions at the critical regularity threshold. The proof introduces a new infinite hierarchy of equations with a cleaner linear structure than the standard Gross-Pitaevskii hierarchy.

math.AP
Original abstract

We study the quadratic nonlinear Schrödinger equation in energy-critical and energy-supercritical regimes and establish unconditional uniqueness at critical regularity on both $\mathbb{T}^{d}$ and $\mathbb{R}^{d}$. We introduce a new infinite quadratic hierarchy that featuring a linear structure for tensor product forms, instead of marginal densities. Consequently, this newly constructed hierarchy differs from the quantum Gross-Pitaevskii hierarchy, and its structure is in fact closer to that of the classical Boltzmann hierarchy. We prove this quadratic hierarchy admits combinatorial structures that are compatible with the bilinear $U$-$V$ estimates we prove for the quadratic nonlinearity. These tools enable us to establish unconditional uniqueness at the critical regularity via a quadratic hierarchy approach, and thus to provide an affirmative answer to Bourgain's uniqueness concern [3,p.152] for the quadratic nonlinearity in the weak-form bilinear case.

Nonlocal minimal surfaces are generically unique, and smooth in one extra dimension

Serena Dipierro, Xavier Fernández-Real, Enrico Valdinoci

Nonlocal minimal surfaces minimize a fractional notion of perimeter and can behave wildly — failing to be unique or smooth in high dimensions. This paper proves that non-uniqueness is genuinely rare: within any one-parameter family of boundary conditions, it occurs for at most countably many values. Furthermore, small perturbations to the boundary data can always improve interior smoothness by one dimension.

math.AP
Original abstract

We study the fractional Plateau problem for $s$-minimal sets with prescribed exterior datum. For fixed exterior data, minimizers need not be unique, and singularities may occur beyond the critical dimension. We first prove a generic uniqueness theorem: along any strictly increasing family of exterior data, nonuniqueness occurs for at most countably many parameters. We then show that one can make arbitrarily small perturbations for which the interior regularity theory improves by one dimension.

Boundedness and blow-up for a quasilinear Keller-Segel system with flux limitation and indirect signal production

Yue Zhou, Changchun Liu

math.AP
Original abstract

The quasilinear Keller-Segel system with flux limitation and indirect signal production u_t=\nabla\cdot\left(D(u)\nabla u\right) -\nabla\cdot\left(u(1+\left|\nabla v\right|^2)^σ\nabla v\right), &x\inΩ, t>0, \\ 0=Δv-v+w,x\inΩ, t>0, \\ w_t=-w+u,x\inΩ, t>0, under homogeneous Neumann boundary conditions in a smooth bounded domain Ω\subsetR^N is considered, where D(u)\simeq u^{m-1} as u\simeq\infty. We conclude that For N=1 and any σ\in\mathbb{R}, if m\geq0, the classical solution exists globally, and it is moreover bounded if m>0. However, if m<0 and Ωis a ball, there exist radially symmetric initial data such that the classical solution exhibits finite-time blow-up. For any N\geq2 and m>1-\frac{1}{N}, if σ\leq \frac{mN+2-2N}{2N-2}, the classical solution is global. Furthermore, if σ<\frac{mN+2-2N}{2N-2}, the corresponding solution is uniformly bounded.
