For any N\geq2 and m<2-\frac{2}{N}, if σ>\max\left\{\frac{N}{2-2N},\frac{mN+2-2N}{2N-2}\right\} and Ωis a ball, there exist radially symmetric initial data such that the classical solution blows up in finite time.

On the Gross-Pitaevskii model with a moving impurity: Cauchy problem and superfluidity criterion

Paolo Antonelli, Martino Caliaro

Can a quantum fluid flow past an obstacle without friction — a hallmark of superfluidity — and can we make this mathematically rigorous? This paper studies the 1D Gross-Pitaevskii equation (a PDE modeling quantum fluids like Bose-Einstein condensates) with a moving point-like impurity, proving the equation is globally well-posed and identifying a critical speed below which stable, drag-free flow exists. The work gives a precise mathematical foundation for the superfluidity criterion.

math.AP math-ph
Original abstract

We study the one-dimensional Gross-Pitaevskii equation with a traveling delta potential and non-zero conditions at infinity. This model describes the effect of a moving impurity in a quantum fluid. Firstly, we show that the associated Cauchy problem is globally well-posed in the energy space. This requires the definition of a conserved energy, which involves the notion of renormalized momentum. Secondly, we study the existence and stability of stationary states in a co-moving reference frame. It is known that there exists an impurity-dependent critical velocity above which no stationary state exists. For velocities below the critical one, two different stationary states appear. We show the orbital stability of the one with higher minimal density.

On a zero-mass $(p,q)$-Laplacian equation involving subcritical and supercritical growth in $\mathbb R^N$

Everaldo Souto De Medeiros, Antonio Araújo Do Nascimento, Gaetano Siciliano

When does the PDE involving both p-Laplacian and q-Laplacian operators (nonlinear generalizations of the ordinary Laplacian) have solutions on all of ℝᴺ without a mass term anchoring them? This paper maps out exactly when solutions exist or don't, showing the sign of the parameter λ is decisive, even when the nonlinear exponents cross the critical Sobolev threshold (the exponent beyond which standard compactness arguments break down). The proofs use variational methods adapted to this mixed-operator setting.

math.AP
Original abstract

This paper is concerned with the zero-mass $(p,q)$-Laplacian equation $$ -Δ_p u-Δ_q u = |u|^{r-2}u+λ|u|^{s-2}u \quad \text{in } \mathbb{R}^N, $$ where $1<q<p<N$. The exponents $r$ and $s$ may belong to either the subcritical or the supercritical range with respect to the critical Sobolev exponents $p^*$ and $q^*$. We establish existence and nonexistence results and show that the sign of the parameter $λ$ determines the solvability regimes of the equation. The existence proofs rely on variational methods, truncation arguments, and regularity theory, while the nonexistence results are derived from a suitable Pohozaev-type identity for the $(p,q)$-Laplacian operator.

Linear Stability and Jacobi Kernels of Three-Dimensional Sessile Drops

Xiaoding Yang

A sessile drop is a liquid droplet resting on a surface, shaped by gravity and surface tension. This paper analyzes when such 3D axisymmetric drops are stable by studying the second variation of their energy and the resulting Jacobi equation — a differential equation whose solutions reveal neutral stability modes. The key challenge is that horizontal translations create unavoidable degeneracy, and the paper carefully characterizes exactly which perturbations lie in this kernel.

math.AP math.DG
Original abstract

We consider the linear stability of three-dimensional sessile drops with a free contact line. The equilibrium surface is an axisymmetric solution of the Young--Laplace equation with gravity, fixed volume, and prescribed contact angle. We derive the constrained second variation of the gravity--capillary energy and formulate the associated Jacobi problem. Although variational stability gives nonnegativity, the second variation is necessarily degenerate because horizontal translations preserve the energy. Our main result identifies this degeneracy completely. Under the pressure--volume nondegeneracy condition $(dV/dλ\neq 0)$, we prove that the kernel of the constrained Jacobi operator is exactly the two-dimensional space generated by horizontal translations. The proof combines the geometric structure of the Jacobi operator with a Fourier-mode analysis: the axisymmetric mode is ruled out by the pressure--volume condition, the first mode gives translations, and all higher modes are excluded by comparison. This provides the precise linear nondegeneracy underlying stability of droplet dynamics modulo translations.

Gradient continuity for $p$-Laplacian obstacle problems under mean oscillation conditions

Sungjin Lee, Jihoon Ok

The p-Laplacian obstacle problem asks for the smoothest function above a given barrier ('obstacle') satisfying a generalized Laplace equation. This paper proves that solutions have continuous gradients (C¹-regularity) under a Dini mean oscillation condition — a weaker assumption than classical Dini continuity — on the equation's coefficients, source, and obstacle. This broadens the class of problems known to have smooth solutions.

math.AP
Original abstract

We establish the $C^1$-regularity of solutions to the obstacle problems associated with $p$-Laplacian type equations, where $1<p<\infty$. Specifically, we prove that the gradient of the solution is continuous under a Dini mean oscillation ($\mathsf{DMO}$) type condition on the data, which includes the coefficient matrix, the source term, and the obstacle function. This result relaxes the classical Dini continuity assumption on the data to a more general mean oscillation condition.

Robust Observability for Schrödinger Equations with Rough Potentials on 2D Compact Hyperbolic Surfaces

Jiayan Wu, Ting Zhang, Ruze Zhou

Observability for the Schrödinger equation asks whether measuring a solution on a small region of a surface for finite time determines the full initial state. This paper proves that on compact hyperbolic surfaces — negatively curved spaces with chaotic geodesic flow — this estimate remains valid even after adding rough L² potential perturbations, exploiting the strong quantum ergodicity that hyperbolic geometry provides.

math.AP
Original abstract

This paper investigates the robustness of quantum observability on compact hyperbolic surfaces under $L^2$ potential perturbations. Since $L^2$ regularity is strictly subcritical in dimension two, for every non-empty open set $Ω\subset M$ and $T>0$, the solution of $(i\partial_t+Δ-V)u=0$ satisfies the space-time observability estimate $$ \|u_0\|_{L^2(M)}^2 \leq C\int_0^T \|e^{-it(-Δ+V)}u_0\|_{L^2(Ω)}^2\,dt. $$ Our results provide a quantitative confirmation that the delocalization of high-energy quantum states on negatively curved manifolds is robust against $L^2$-class microscopic scattering. The proof combines the hyperbolic dynamics of the geodesic flow with semiclassical analysis. A key ingredient is an $L^4$ spectral cluster estimate with an arbitrarily small loss, obtained by exploiting Jacobi field analysis and Bourgain--Demeter $l^2$-decoupling. This estimate allows us to obtain refined spectral localized semiclassical Strichartz estimates adapted to rough potentials and to show that the potential's contribution vanishes in the propagation of semiclassical measures. The full-support property of invariant semiclassical measures on hyperbolic surfaces then yields the desired observability by contradiction. By the Hilbert Uniqueness Method, the corresponding internal controllability result follows.

Nonlinear stability of a background magnetic field for the 3D compressible MHD equations with anisotropic dissipation

Jincheng Gao, Xianpeng Hu, Lianyun Peng et al.

Magnetohydrodynamics (MHD) couples fluid flow with magnetic fields, and a key question is whether small perturbations near a steady magnetic background stay small forever. This paper proves global nonlinear stability for 3D compressible MHD with a highly anisotropic dissipation—friction only in horizontal directions, magnetic diffusion in just one direction—showing the solution decays to equilibrium. This minimal-dissipation result demonstrates that background magnetic fields have a genuine stabilizing effect even when viscosity is severely limited.

math.AP
Original abstract

We study the nonlinear stability of an equilibrium with a background magnetic field for the three-dimensional compressible magnetohydrodynamic (MHD) equations in the whole space $\mathbb{R}^3$, in the strongly anisotropic regime where the velocity is dissipated only in the horizontal directions and the magnetic field is diffused in a single direction. We prove that for initial data sufficiently close to the equilibrium in a Sobolev space, the system admits a unique global-in-time solution that remains close to the equilibrium and enjoys quantitative dissipation estimates. The proof overcomes the severe lack of dissipation through two mechanisms: the background magnetic field is shown to generate enhanced dissipation for the magnetic field and the density, while a nonlinear cancellation mechanism is devised to resolve the loss of vertical derivatives caused by the compressible coupling.

Power Convexity of Solutions to the Complex Monge-Ampère Equation $\det(u_{i\overline{j}})=1$ in Complex Dimension Two

Hongyu Chen, Jingchen Hu, Li Sheng

Can solutions to a key equation in complex geometry inherit 'convexity' properties from their domain? This paper proves that solutions to the complex Monge-Ampère equation — which governs how volume distorts under complex coordinate changes — on convex domains in two complex dimensions are 'power convex' (meaning some power of the solution is convex in the classical sense). The result advances understanding of geometric structure in complex analysis, with a method designed to generalize to higher dimensions.

math.AP
Original abstract

In this paper, we establish the power convexity of solutions to the complex Monge-Ampère equation $\det(u_{i\overline{j}})=1$ in a convex set in the complex 2 dimensional Euclidean space. Even the convexity result is only for 2 dimension, our approach is applicable to all dimensions.

$H$-convergence and $Γ$-convergence in the Riesz fractional setting: the nonlinear case

Giuseppe C. Brusca, Maicol Caponi, Alessandro Carbotti et al.

When you replace ordinary derivatives with fractional (nonlocal) ones, do the classical homogenization theories still work? This paper shows that H-convergence—a tool for averaging highly oscillatory PDEs—behaves identically in the Riesz fractional gradient setting as in the classical local setting, and extends this to Γ-convergence for energy functionals. This unifies local and nonlocal variational theories and provides compactness results essential for studying materials with long-range interactions.

math.AP
Original abstract

This paper concerns the $H$-convergence of nonlinear nonlocal monotone operators defined through the Riesz fractional gradient and divergence. We show that the $H$-convergence in this nonlocal framework is equivalent to the $H$-convergence of the corresponding local one. As a consequence, we obtain a $H$-compactness result for a suitable class of nonlocal monotone operators. We then study the $Γ$-convergence of nonlocal energy functionals associated with the subclass of \emph{conservative} monotone operators, proving that it is equivalent to the $Γ$-convergence of the corresponding local energies. A key ingredient is a new uniqueness result for the integral representation of both local and nonlocal functionals. As a by-product, we obtain the $Γ$-compactness of the class of nonlocal energies under consideration. Finally, we show the equivalence between the $H$-convergence of nonlocal conservative monotone operators and the $Γ$-convergence of the associated energy functionals.

On the image of the scattering map for horizon-regular solutions of the linear scalar wave equation on the Schwarzschild black hole exterior

Hamed Masaood

When waves scatter off a Schwarzschild black hole (the simplest black hole spacetime), radiation can escape to infinity or fall through the event horizon. This paper precisely characterizes which pairs of radiation patterns — one at infinity, one at the horizon — can arise from physically regular initial data, constructing explicit Hilbert-space isomorphisms between these spaces. The result sharpens our mathematical understanding of black hole scattering theory and what information is accessible to distant observers.

gr-qc math-ph math.AP
Original abstract

We construct Hilbert-space isomorphisms identifying the spaces of radiation fields on the event horizon and null infinity that are induced by the forward scattering map on the Schwarzschild exterior for fixed spherical harmonic mode solutions to the linear scalar wave equation arising from Cauchy data sets at $t=0$ which are regular at the event horizon, in the sense that the local energy at the event horizon is finite. We show that fixed spherical harmonic mode solutions with no radiation on the event horizon must decay exponentially in time, at a rate that is determined by the surface gravity. On the other hand, we construct examples of fixed spherical harmonic mode solutions with no radiation on null infinity and which have polynomial decay along the event horizon. Finally, we construct examples of polynomially decaying solutions to the linear scalar wave equation which are regular at the event horizon, have unbounded support in spherical harmonic modes, and induce no radiation on the event horizon.

A compensated Piola principle for critical nondiffusive parabolic systems

Maotuo Guo

Many fluid and parabolic PDE systems are hard to analyze because nonlinear terms exceed what standard function-space estimates allow — the so-called 'below product threshold' regime. This paper introduces a new geometric technique called the compensated Piola graph method to handle exactly this difficulty, establishing well-posedness (existence, uniqueness, and stability of solutions) in critical Besov spaces for a broad class of incompressible parabolic systems coupled to non-diffusing internal variables.

math.AP
Original abstract

We introduce a compensated Piola graph method for Lagrangian stability estimates in critical homogeneous Besov spaces below the usual product threshold. For $d\ge2$, $2d\le p<\infty$, and $s=d/p$, we establish local Hadamard well-posedness and a continuation criterion in the scaling-critical Besov phase space $\dot B^{s-1}_{p,1}(\mathbb R^d)^d\times \dot B^s_{p,1}(\mathbb R^d)$, for a class of incompressible parabolic systems coupled to nondiffusive internal variables. The main obstruction is that the formal Piola product between the inverse deformation gradient and the Lagrangian velocity is only borderline at $p=2d$ and is not continuous in general for $p>2d$. We replace this product by a closed solenoidal Piola graph, using a compensated divergence structure that survives in the high-integrability range. The principle applies to viscous non-resistive MHD, Hookean incompressible viscoelasticity, and nondiffusive Oldroyd--B systems with affine objective terms. In particular, it closes the previously untreated high-$p$ Hadamard well-posedness range for critical non-resistive MHD and for the nondiffusive Oldroyd--B systems considered here; combined with the known low-$p$ MHD theory, it gives the finite-$p$, $q=1$ critical Besov picture for non-resistive MHD.

Sharp ratios for low-index Neumann eigenvalues on convex domains

Quanyu Tang, Haiqi Zhang

The Neumann eigenvalues of the Laplacian on a domain measure the natural vibration frequencies under 'free boundary' conditions. For any bounded convex region in any dimension, this paper proves the sharp inequalities μ₂ ≤ 4μ₁ and μ₃ ≤ 9μ₁, resolving a longstanding open problem attributed to Henrot. The constants can't be improved, confirming that intervals (one-dimensional domains) are the extremal cases.

math.AP math.SP
Original abstract

Let $Ω\subset\mathbb{R}^N$ be a bounded open convex set, and let $0=μ_0(Ω)<μ_1(Ω)\le μ_2(Ω)\le\cdots$ be the Neumann eigenvalues of the Laplacian, repeated according to multiplicity. We prove the sharp bounds $$ μ_2(Ω)\le 4μ_1(Ω),\qquad μ_3(Ω)\le 9μ_1(Ω). $$ The first estimate resolves a problem attributed to Henrot, while the second gives the next sharp case predicted by the one-dimensional model. The constants are optimal in every dimension.

Sharp decay estimates for global solutions to the incompressible rotating Navier--Stokes equations

Mikihiro Fujii, Yang Li, Jiang Xu

The rotating Navier–Stokes equations model fluid flow under Coriolis forces (as in geophysical flows), and a central question is how quickly global solutions decay in Lp norms over time. This paper establishes sharp decay rates for all 2<p<∞, showing they are strictly faster than what interpolation between the L2 and L∞ endpoint estimates would suggest—closing a gap in the theory by proving both upper and lower bounds.

math.AP
Original abstract

In this paper, we consider the three-dimensional incompressible rotating Navier--Stokes equations and establish the sharp $L^p$ decay estimates of global solutions. We reveal that the optimal $L^p$ decay rates for $2<p<\infty$ are strictly faster than those obtained in existing results by interpolation between the $L^2$ unitary identity and $L^\infty$ dispersive estimates, although the endpoint cases were known to be sharp. Moreover, the optimality of decay rates is also proved by the lower bound estimate for a specific initial datum. The underlying mechanism lies in the anisotropic degeneracy of the oscillatory integrals arising from the Coriolis force.

Classification of singular solutions to the subcritical Lane-Emden equation in $\mathbb{R}^N \setminus \{0\}$

Francesco Esposito, Luigi Muglia, Berardino Sciunzi et al.

The Lane-Emden equation is a nonlinear PDE appearing in astrophysics and geometry; a 'singular solution' blows up at the origin rather than being smoothly extendable there. This paper fully classifies all positive singular solutions in the subcritical parameter range, determining exactly what behavior is possible near the singularity. Such classification results are fundamental for understanding solution structure and ruling out pathological behavior in nonlinear PDE theory.

math.AP
Original abstract

We provide a classification result of positive solutions with a non-removable singularity at the origin to the subcritical Lane-Emden equation $-Δ_p u = u^q$ in $\mathbb{R}^N \setminus \{0\}$, where $p\in(1,N)$, $q\in (p-1,p^*-1)$ and $u^{q-p+1} \in L^{\frac{N}{p}}(\mathbb{R}^N \setminus B_R)$.

Nonexistence of blow-up solutions with smooth radiation for energy-critical equivariant wave maps

Jacek Jendrej, Yuchen Yin, Lifeng Zhao

Energy-critical wave maps are solutions to a geometric wave equation mapping spacetime into a sphere, and 'blow-up' means the solution develops a singularity in finite time. This paper proves that for equivariance degree k≥2, any blowing-up solution must have irregular (non-smooth) radiation escaping to infinity — ruling out a whole class of 'clean' blow-up scenarios that do exist for k=1. The proof introduces a novel use of the Funk–Hecke theorem within the soliton resolution framework.

math.AP
Original abstract

We study $k$-equivariant energy critical wave maps $\mathbb{R}^{1+2} \to \mathbb{S}^2$, for any equivariance degree $k\ge 2$. We prove that the radiation associated with any finite-energy blow-up solution cannot satisfy a certain regularity condition; in particular, it cannot be smooth. The assumption $k \geq 2$ is necessary, since for $k = 1$ such solutions are known to exist. The starting point of our analysis is the soliton resolution theorem. The key ingredient is a novel application of the modulation method, in which we compare the effects of the radiation and inner bubbles to study the dynamic behavior of the widest bubble.

A note on new mapping properties for Wolff potential

Petteri Harjulehto, Ritva Hurri-Syrjänen

The Wolff potential is a nonlinear integral operator central to potential theory, and Choquet integrals replace ordinary integration using fractal-like Hausdorff content instead of measure. This note establishes new integrability estimates for the Wolff potential in this setting and applies them to prove regularity of solutions to the p-Laplace equation — a nonlinear PDE generalizing classical harmonic functions.

math.AP math.FA
Original abstract

We study integrability properties of the Wolff potential in context of Choquet integrals with respect to the Hausdorff content. As an application we give integrability results to the solutions of the $p$-Laplace equation in this context.

Global solutions to the Navier--Stokes equations with large vertical velocities in $\dot{B}_{\infty,σ}^{-1}(\mathbb{R}^3)$

Mikihiro Fujii

The 3D Navier-Stokes equations are notoriously hard to solve globally; this paper shows unique global solutions exist when the horizontal velocity is small in critical Besov spaces (function spaces measuring smoothness and decay) even if the vertical velocity is arbitrarily large in a broader space where the equations are known to be ill-posed. This cleverly exploits the anisotropic structure of 3D fluid flow, pushing past a major barrier in the global well-posedness problem.

math.AP
Original abstract

In this paper, we consider the Cauchy problem for the $3$D incompressible Navier--Stokes equations and prove the existence of unique global solutions in the framework that the horizontal component of the velocity field is small in some critical Besov spaces including the classical Fujita--Kato class, while the vertical component is large in the wide class $\dot{B}_{\infty,σ}^{-1}(\mathbb{R}^3)$ ($1 \leq σ< \infty$) where the Navier--Stokes equations are known to be ill-posed in.

Thresholds, fragmentation and symmetrization in parabolic equations

Matthieu Alfaro, Yihong Du, François Hamel et al.

Bistable reaction-diffusion equations model phenomena like population spread, where solutions either die out or persist long-term. This paper precisely characterizes the threshold initial conditions separating these two fates for interval-shaped initial data, and studies how fragmented or asymmetric initial data behaves at large times—showing in particular that solutions can symmetrize even from highly irregular starts.

math.AP
Original abstract

This paper is mainly concerned with the large-time dynamics of bounded nonnegative solutions of reaction-diffusion equations on the real line with nonlinearities mainly of the bistable-type. We first consider initial data of the type $α\mathbf{1}_I$, namely scalar multiples of indicator functions of intervals $I$. For each amplitude $α$, the existence of a threshold length $L^*(α)$ separating the extinction and the persistence of the solutions at large time is known. We here address the question as to whether the limit of the threshold sizes $L^*(α)$ as $α\to+\infty$ is positive or zero. We provide sufficient conditions under which this limit is positive, and others under which it is zero. Secondly, when the limit is positive, we show that some fragmented initial data, which are equally distributed as $α\mathbf{1}_I$, give rise to solutions persisting at large time, whereas the solutions emanating from $α\mathbf{1}_I$ go to extinction at large time. This result shows an unexpected favourable effect of the fragmentation of the initial datum on the large-time dynamics. Last, we consider the mass concentration principle for parabolic equations, which states that nonnegative solutions can be controlled from above in an integral sense by the solutions emanating from the Schwarz symmetrically decreasing rearrangements of the initial data. Since the pioneering results of [Bandle, 1976] and [Alvino, Trombetti, Lions, 1990], this principle is known to hold in bounded domains with Dirichlet boundary conditions under different types of assumptions of the coefficients of the equation. We show that this mass concentration principle is not valid in general, even for simple equations of the type $\partial_t u = \partial_{xx} u + f(u)$.

Time-periodic solutions of the Boltzmann equation with soft potentials in $\mathbb{R}^3$

Yuanjie Lei, Shuangqian Liu, Jiaying Zhang et al.

The Boltzmann equation models gas dynamics, and 'soft potentials' describe particles with weaker long-range interactions, making analysis harder due to a missing 'spectral gap' — a mathematical property that normally guarantees solutions stabilize. This paper proves that time-periodic solutions exist and are stable across all soft potential cases, using a sophisticated blend of Besov and Sobolev function-space techniques combined with velocity-weighted energy estimates.

math.AP
Original abstract

We establish the existence of time-periodic solutions to the Boltzmann equation for the full range of soft potentials and prove their global stability. The proof relies on refined energy estimates combining Besov and Sobolev regularity with velocity-weighted energy methods. The analysis is complicated by the lack of a spectral gap in the soft potential regime together with the absence of time integrability of the periodic forcing over $\mathbb{R}^+$. This provides a framework for the study of time-periodic behavior for the Boltzmann equation.

Gevrey instability in the inviscid inflow-outflow problem

Igor Kukavica, Wojciech Ożański, Qi Xu

The 2D Euler equations govern ideal (inviscid) fluid flow, and this paper studies them on a channel where fluid flows in and out through the boundary. The key result is that the problem is well-posed in Gevrey spaces — function spaces slightly larger than analytic functions, capturing near-analytic smoothness — but genuinely ill-posed in standard Sobolev spaces, pinpointing exactly how much regularity the inflow-outflow boundary condition demands.

math.AP
Original abstract

We consider the 2D incompressible Euler equations on a periodic channel $\mathbb{T}\times (0,1)$ with inflow-outflow boundary condition $u=(0,1)$ on $\mathbb{T} \times \{0,1 \}$. We also impose the incoming vorticity boundary condition $ω=η$ on $\mathbb{T}\times \{ 0 \}$, where $η$ is prescribed. We show that the problem is globally well-posed in Gevrey spaces (for any value of the Gevrey exponent $s>1$) as long as $η$ remains Gevrey. This proves that the inflow-outflow velocity boundary condition determines the solution locally in time if and only if the solution is considered in an analytic class. In particular, leaving the analytic class, nonuniquness of solutions occurs already in any Gevrey class, by prescribing $η$. Hence, prescribing an analytic inflow-outflow velocity leads to precisely one analytic and continuum $s$-Gevrey solutions for every $s>1$. Furthermore, the result implies that if $η$ is analytic, then the unique global solution can lose analyticity in space for all $t>0$, but remain $s$-Gevrey regular for all $s$.

Global Calderon-Zygmund estimates for irregular double-phase evolution problem with non-divergence data

Rakesh Arora, Sergey Shmarev

This paper proves sharp regularity estimates for parabolic 'double-phase' equations, where the nonlinearity switches between two different power laws depending on location. The main result establishes Calderón–Zygmund estimates—meaning the gradient of the solution is as integrable as the data allows—even when the domain is irregular and the exponents vary, extending classical elliptic theory to this more complex setting.

math.AP
Original abstract

We study irregular double-phase parabolic equations with variable exponents and non-divergence data, \[ u_t-\operatorname{div} \left(\mathcal{F}(z,\nabla u)\nabla u \right)=f(z),\quad z=(x,t)\in Q_T:=Ω\times (0,T), \] under the homogeneous Dirichlet boundary conditions. Here, $Ω\subset \mathbb{R}^N$, $N \geq 2$, is a bounded domain, $T>0$, \[ \mathcal{F}(z,\nabla u)=a(z)|\nabla u|^{p(z)-2} + b(z) |\nabla u |^{q(z)-2} \] with given Lipschitz-continuous exponents $p,q$ that satisfy a suitable balance condition. The nonnegative coefficients $a(z), b(z)$ satisfy the inequality $a(z)+b(z)>0$ in $Q_T$, the space and time derivatives of $a$ and $b$ belong to $L^d(Q_T)$ with some $d$ depending on the data. If \[ f\in L^σ(Q_T) \quad \text{for} \ σ\in (2, N+2] \quad \text{and} \quad \mathcal{F}((\cdot,0),\nabla u_0)\,|\nabla u_0|^{r+2}\in L^1(Ω), \] where \(0\le r\le K(N,σ,p,q)\) if \(σ<N+2\), while \(r\ge0\) is arbitrary if \(σ=N+2\), then the problem has a unique strong solution, for which we prove the global transfer of integrability from the initial data and the forcing term to the double-phase flux in the spirit of Calderón-Zygmund theory, higher integrability of the gradient, and the second-order space regularity: \[ \begin{split} & \text{$\mathcal{F}((\cdot,t),\nabla u(\cdot,t))|\nabla u(\cdot,t)|^{r+2}\in L^1(Ω)$ for a.e. $t\in (0,T)$}, \\ & \text{$|\nabla u|^{2(\min\{p(z),q(z)\}-1)+r+s}\in L^1(Q_T)$ for every $s\in\left(0,\frac{4}{N+2}\right)$}, \\ & \mathcal{F}(z,\nabla u)|\nabla u|^{\frac{r+2}{2}} \in L^2(0,T;W^{1,2}(Ω)). \end{split} \] The results improve and complement the results in \cite{Arora-Shmarev-JGA-2026} and extend them to the full range $r \geq 0$.

Classification of solutions to an $n^{\text{th}}$ order conformally invariant elliptic equation on $\mathbb R^n$ with nonlocal nonlinearity

Mathew Gluck, Janani Marasinghe

Can we fully classify all solutions to a key equation in conformal geometry when a nonlocal interaction term is added? This paper answers yes: under natural energy and growth conditions, all solutions to a Choquard-type generalization of the constant Q-curvature equation (which governs how curvature behaves under angle-preserving maps) on ℝⁿ are explicitly characterized. This resolves the solution classification for a natural nonlocal extension of a central problem in geometric analysis.

math.AP
Original abstract

This paper concerns a conformally invariant elliptic problem on $\mathbb R^n$ driven by $(-Δ)^{n/2}$ that has a nonlocal exponential nonlinearity of Choquard type. The problem under consideration is a nonlocal generalization of the constant $Q$-curvature problem on $\mathbb R^n$. We classify the asymptotic behavior at infinity of all solutions that satisfy a suitable integrability assumption. Under a growth restriction at infinity and a lower bound on the energy we provide an explicit classification for solutions. The classification is heuristically consistent with the classification of the corresponding local problem.

Global Existence and Time Decay for the Vlasov-Hartree System

Kieran Cavanagh

The Vlasov-Hartree system models a quantum-classical mixture of bosons and fermions interacting via mean-field forces. This paper proves the system is globally well-posed (solutions exist for all time without blowup) even for large initial data, and establishes that particle densities spread out and decay over time. This matters because combining quantum and classical descriptions in a single rigorously tractable framework is genuinely difficult, and large-data global results are rare in kinetic theory.

math.AP math-ph
Original abstract

The Vlasov-Hartree system is a mean-field model for a mixture of infinitely many interacting bosons and fermions where the bosons are described quantum mechanically and the fermions are described classically. This paper studies the well-posedness and dispersive properties of the Vlasov-Hartree system with initial data of arbitrary size. We prove that the Vlasov-Hartree system is globally well-posed in a low-regularity functional framework where the particle trajectories are meaningfully defined, but which includes discontinuous fermion densities. The solutions are classical when the initial fermion density is regular enough. Moreover, when the interaction between the bosons and fermions is repulsive, we prove that the system exhibits dispersion in the form of time decay estimates for the particle densities and fields. When the interaction is attractive, we show that, at worst, the fields exhibit very mild growth in time.

The nonlocal attraction-repulsion transport equation with power kernels

Massimo Fornasier, Hui Huang, Lukang Sun

This paper studies how a cloud of particles evolves when simultaneously attracted toward a fixed background and repelled from each other, with both forces following power-law rules. Using a regularization technique, the authors prove global well-posedness — solutions exist for all time and remain well-behaved — and identify exactly which attraction-repulsion balance leads to steady states. This clarifies the long-term fate of aggregation models appearing in biology and physics.

math.AP
Original abstract

We study a nonlocal continuity equation on $\mathbb{R}^d$ in which a probability density is driven by the competition between attraction toward a prescribed background measure $ω$ and self-repulsion among particles, governed respectively by the power-law kernels $ψ_a(x) = |x|^{1+a}$ and $ψ_r(x) = |x|^{1+r}$ with exponents $a, r \in [0,1)$. We establish global Lagrangian well-posedness via a squared-radius regularization, obtaining uniform $L^\infty$ and moment bounds, $W^{n,\infty}$ regularity, and uniqueness in the Lagrangian class. When the initial data is compactly supported and attraction dominates ($a > r$, or $a = r$ with $ω(\mathbb{R}^d) > 1$), we prove that the support remains uniformly bounded at all time; a counterexample shows this fails for $a = r > 1$. For the attractive-dominant nonquadratic range $0 \leq r \leq a < 1$, we characterize zero-flux stationary states via a free-boundary problem involving a fractional Laplacian operator, reducing the stationarity condition to a fractional exterior Dirichlet problem. This characterization allow us to exhibit explicit examples of stationary measures in dimensions $d \in \{1,2,3\}$. Numerical particle simulations confirm agreement with the theoretical stationary profiles. Finally, we prove that every global solution with bounded energy and uniform moment bounds converges to a zero-flux stationary state.

Scattering and Low-Speed Critical Elements for the 3D Focusing Energy-Critical NLS

Pang-Hung Chung, Dan Han

When does a solution to the 3D focusing energy-critical nonlinear Schrödinger equation scatter (spread out like a free wave) rather than concentrate? The paper tackles the 'below-threshold' regime by showing that soliton-like solutions whose concentration centers drift slowly enough must vanish entirely, removing a key obstruction to proving scattering without the usual radial symmetry assumption.

math.AP math.DS
Original abstract

The below-threshold scattering problem is considered for the three-dimensional focusing energy-critical nonlinear Schrödinger equation. A main non-radial obstruction is the possible drift of the concentration center of a soliton-like critical element, which prevents a fixed-center localized virial estimate from closing directly. It is shown that such a compact critical solution must vanish if it has an $L_t^\infty L_x^q$ bound and its center drifts sufficiently slowly. The result covers the pure-energy, endpoint $L^4$, and finite-mass regimes and gives a corresponding conditional scattering criterion.

Exponential growth and decay estimates for eigenfunctions with complex eigenvalues

Kenichi Ito

This paper establishes how fast eigenfunctions of non-self-adjoint Schrödinger operators—quantum mechanical operators where energy eigenvalues can be complex—grow or decay in space. Two natural critical exponents emerge from the imaginary parts of square roots of the eigenvalues, controlling the possible exponential rates. This extends classical tools like the Agmon estimates (which bound decay for real eigenvalues) to the significantly harder complex-eigenvalue setting.

math.AP math-ph math.FA
Original abstract

We prove a priori exponential growth and decay estimates for locally square integrable eigenfunctions with complex eigenvalues of the non-self-adjoint Schrödinger operator. There appear two natural critical exponents, given by the imaginary parts of square roots of twice an eigenvalue. Any associated eigenfunction has the same exponential growth or decay rate as one of these, or greater than the upper one. The results may be seen as extensions of the Phragmén--Lindelöf theorem, the Agmon estimates and Rellich's theorem to complex eigenvalues. For the proofs we adapt a commutator scheme of Ito--Skibsted~(2020) to the non-self-adjoint framework, presenting a unified approach to all of them. In order to control contributions from the imaginary parts of a potential and an eigenvalue, an additional commutator estimate is employed.

Noncommutative Anisotropic Diffusion in Hilbert Space. II. Global Closure of the Logarithmic Gradient, Lower Bounds, and Nanosystem Applications

E. Yu. Shchetinin, A. A. Shevchuk, S. I. Salpagarov

How do diffusion processes—used in generative AI and physics—behave when extended to infinite-dimensional, noncommutative settings like spaces of quantum states? This paper establishes explicit entropy constants and checkable stability bounds for such dynamics in Hilbert space, going beyond mere existence results. The work targets applications to nanosystems where quantum geometry matters and standard commutative probability tools break down.

math.AP math.PR
Original abstract

This second part of the series develops the statistical and applied layer of the theory built in Part I [1]. Unlike current Hilbert diffusion models [2-5], we focus not only on well-posedness of the infinite-dimensional generative dynamics, but on the noncommutative $A$-geometry, explicit entropy constants, and checkable lower bounds. The analytic estimate of Part I reduces stability of the backward evolution to control of a validation error $\mathcal{E}_{val}$. We prove three results. First, we construct a cylindrically weak denoising label for the infinite-dimensional score field, consistent with the exact logarithmic gradient. Second, the local parametric closure is replaced by a global nonparametric score closure, its complexity controlled via the Dudley entropy integral and uniform empirical bounds in $L^2(ν_*;A)$. Third, for the trace-smoothed nonlinear class we construct minimax bounds by the Le Cam-Assouad method, showing the root statistical rate cannot be improved without additional quadratic structure. The final section applies the theory to anisotropic diffusion in a nanosystem model and checks the constants $c_A,C_A,C_{LSI}^A$ independently of any smallness condition, establishing explicit accuracy orders: the parametric trace-smoothed minimax score risk is $p/M$, while the uniform error of the risk functional is $\sqrt{p/M}$, showing the statistical plateau of order $p/M$ is not a proof artifact. The applied layer closes with an independent analytic benchmark for the isotropic case, a comparison with classical cell homogenization, and an approximation theorem for smooth logarithmic gradients by $A$-adapted spectral networks.

Combinatorics

54

Gilbreath's conjecture: a Cramér random model and a deterministic analysis

Zachary Chase, Zach Hunter, Terence Tao

Gilbreath's conjecture says that repeatedly taking absolute differences of the prime sequence always leaves a 1 in the leftmost column—a pattern observed but unproven since 1958. This paper proves the conjecture holds for a probabilistic model replacing prime gaps with independent geometric random variables, and adds deterministic partial analysis. Verifying the conjecture in a well-accepted random model (the Cramér model) provides strong heuristic evidence and a rigorous framework for understanding why the pattern persists.

math.CO
Original abstract

Gilbreath's conjecture asserts that if one starts with the sequence of primes and takes successive absolute differences to create a triangular array, then the left diagonal of this array consists entirely of ones after the first row. In this paper, we show that the analogue of this conjecture for a Cramér random model holds, in which the (normalized) prime gaps are replaced by independent random variables with geometric distributions of logarithmic size. We also give some preliminary analysis of the associated continuous probabilistic model for this problem, as well as a deterministic "inverse theorem" that isolates the specific obstructions to Gilbreath's conjecture (assuming a Cramér type bound on prime gaps), namely long blocks of zeroes, or very long shallow $\{0,d\}$-valued blocks for some $d \geq 2$.

Twofold universality of large-$N$ melonic random tensors

Carlos I. Perez-Sanchez

Integrating over random tensors—multi-index arrays generalizing matrices—is vastly harder than matrix integration because you need extra combinatorial data to contract indices. This paper constructs a measure under which, at large N, any tensor integral reduces to the same class of 'melonic' Feynman diagrams, establishing a striking double universality. The result dramatically simplifies computations in tensor models relevant to quantum gravity and combinatorics.

math.CO cond-mat.stat-mech hep-th math-ph math.PR
Original abstract

We construct a measure that exhibits two aspects of a new type of universality and dramatically simplifies the integration of tensors $T_{a_1,a_2,\ldots,a_D} \in \mathbb{C}$ ($a_1,\ldots,a_D=1,\ldots,N$) at large $N$. In contrast to matrix integration, in which matrix traces canonically yield the integrand, tensors need additional information (equivalent to a $D$-coloured graph $B$) to contract their indices and form a tensor trace $B(T)$. We show that, whenever each $B_1,\ldots, B_n$ can be obtained by a recursive construction known as melonicity, then the leading order in $N$ of the integral of $ {B_1}(T) {B_2}(T) \cdots {B_n}(T) $ is independent of the -- often intricate -- combinatorics of the traces $B_i$, but also, to our surprise, independent of $D$ as far as $D\geq 3$. Instead, at large $N$, these integrals are some functions (indexed by $n$) of the number of vertices $2p_i$ of $B_i$ which we call melonic polynomials. Melonic traces cumulants with respect to any ('interacting') measure \[ \exp\Big\{-N^{D-1} \sum_{i=1}^m g_i {B_i}(T)\Big\} \mathrm{d}μ_0(T) \quad (g_1,\ldots,g_m \in \mathbb{R}, \mathrm{d}μ_0(T) =\text{the tensor Gaussian}) \] with each $B_i$ melonic, can be computed with our universal measure that replaces each $B_i$ by a canonical trace depending only on $p_i$. We prove that any two melonic tensor models are indistinguishable at large-$N$, independently of the number of tensor indices (first universality aspect), and of the fine-grainedness of their interactions (second universality), being a sufficient condition that the couplings (the parameters $g_i$ above) agree and their respective traces are monomials with the same degree in $T$.

Two-block cycles and chromatic number of Hamiltonian digraphs

Ruilin Zheng, Junying Lu, Xiaolin Wang et al.

A digraph's chromatic number measures how many colors are needed so no edge goes from a lower to higher color. This paper resolves a 2007 conjecture by Addario-Berry, Havet, and Thomassé: strongly connected digraphs avoiding certain two-block cycle families have bounded chromatic number, with the bound depending only on the cycle lengths. The result settles a key case in understanding how forbidden substructures control colorability in directed graphs.

math.CO
Original abstract

Let $k$ and $\ell$ be positive integers. The family $C(k,\ell)$ consists of all digraphs obtained from two internally vertex-disjoint directed paths of lengths at least $k$ and $\ell$, respectively, and identifying their initial vertices and their terminal vertices. Addario-Berry, Havet and Thomassé (JCT-B, 2007) asked whether, for any positive integers $k$ and $\ell$ with $k+\ell \ge 4$, the chromatic number $χ(D)$ is at most $k+\ell-1$ for every $C(k,\ell)$-free strongly connected digraph $D$. Let $D$ be a $C(k,\ell)$-free Hamiltonian digraph. Kim, Kim, Ma and Park (JGT, 2018) showed that $χ(D) \le k+\ell$ and the bound is attained when $k+\ell=5$. In this paper, we prove that $χ(D) \le k+\ell-1$ for $k+\ell\ge 6$ and this bound is best possible for all $k+\ell\geq 6$, which resolves the problem posed by Addario-Berry, Havet and Thomassé for Hamiltonian digraphs.

Perfect Matchings with Prescribed Differences Beyond Hall: The Two-Hole Problem

Aryeh Lev Zabokritskiy

Can you always find a perfect matching in a graph whose edge-differences follow a prescribed pattern? The BGS conjecture says yes for certain algebraic configurations over F₂. This paper tackles the first case beyond the well-understood 'Hall hyperplane' setting—where exactly two prescribed differences are 'internal'—proving the conjecture holds there and revealing unexpected combinatorial complexity even in this minimal generalization.

math.CO
Original abstract

The Balister--Győri--Schelp (BGS) conjecture asks whether every zero-sum list of $2^{s-1}$ nonzero vectors in $\mathbb{F}_2^s$ is the prescribed-difference profile of a perfect matching. The conjecture remains open in general, whereas the classical Hall hyperplane case is solved when all prescribed differences cross between two affine copies of a hyperplane. We isolate the smallest mixed case beyond Hall: exactly two prescribed differences are internal. Although only two requests have changed type, the complete Hall permutation is replaced by a prescribed-difference bijection between two punctured copies of the hyperplane, with two unknown deleted vertices on each side. We call this the two-hole problem. We develop a new combinatorial method for prescribed-difference matchings, based on counting and the character structure of the binary vector space. Unlike the known Hall-type methods, which construct a matching through a sequence of local algorithmic choices, our approach proves existence through a global noncancellation phenomenon. This loss of algorithmic structure is compensated by a different advantage: the method can retain global boundary information that local exchanges do not control. As a first application, it gives a new proof of the binary Hall theorem, and it then yields a complete solution of the two-hole problem with no multiplicity assumption. We also give direct constructive proofs for symmetric even-multiplicity two-hole and four-hole families. More broadly, the new technique provides a framework for studying further subfamilies of the BGS problem by measuring how far their matching structure departs from the Hall case.

Sandpile groups of random bipartite graphs and families of distributions with the same moments

Jason Fulman, Nathan Kaplan, Deepesh Singhal et al.

The 'sandpile group' of a graph is a finite abelian group encoding how chip-firing dynamics balance on that graph; understanding its distribution over random graphs is an active area. This paper constructs large families of distinct probability distributions on finite abelian groups that share identical moments, showing the moment method — a powerful tool for identifying such distributions uniquely — fundamentally fails for certain random bipartite graph models.

math.CO math.NT math.PR
Original abstract

Recently, there has been significant interest in applying the method of moments developed by Wood and others to study distributions of finite abelian groups that arise in number theory and combinatorics. When the moments do not grow too fast, they determine a unique distribution. We construct large families of distributions that have the same moments. These families include several distributions that arise naturally in the study of sandpile groups of families of random graphs. Wood determined the distribution of Sylow $p$-subgroups of sandpile groups of Erdős--Rényi random graphs. This was extended by Mészáros to sandpile groups of random $d$-regular graphs, who observed an interesting special case when $d$ is even and $p = 2$. We study Sylow $p$-subgroups of sandpile groups of random bipartite graphs and similarly find a special case for $p =2$. Although this distribution differs from that of Mészáros, we show that they have the same moments and fit into our broader construction. To compute the moments of the distributions we study, we apply combinatorial tools from the theory of Hall--Littlewood functions.

A note on the shortest law for the symmetric group

Adrian Beker, Luka Milićević, Rudi Mrazović

A 'law' for a group is a word in variables that evaluates to the identity for every substitution; the shortest such word for the symmetric group S_n measures how 'complex' S_n is. The previous best lower bound on this length was roughly 2n; this note improves it to 5n/2, a clean 25% gain achieved via a sharper combinatorial argument.

math.CO math.GR
Original abstract

Let $α(n)$ denote the length of the shortest non-trivial two-variable law for the symmetric group $S_n$. Buskin's quantitative subgroup-separability argument gives the classical lower bound $α(n)\geq 2n-O(1)$. In this short note we give an improvement by proving that $α(n)\geq \frac52 n-O(1)$.

Orlik--Solomon sheaf homology of geometric lattices

Ye Liu

A geometric lattice encodes the intersection structure of a hyperplane arrangement; its Orlik–Solomon algebra is a key algebraic invariant. This paper attaches a 'sheaf' (a tool for tracking local data across a space) to this algebra and computes its homology, finding it concentrates in a single degree with a clean factored structure. Applied to uniform matroids, this yields new representations of symmetric groups.

math.CO
Original abstract

We associate the Orlik--Solomon sheaf with a finite geometric lattice and compute its sheaf homology. We show that this homology concentrates in top degree, admitting a convolution-type decomposition into a principal ideal OS piece tensoring with a principal filter complement poset homology. Applications to uniform matroids provide interesting representations of symmetric groups.

Multiplicity of negative one of independence polynomials of graphs

Om Prakash Bhardwaj, Trung Chau, Sayeed Ikram et al.

The independence polynomial of a graph counts independent sets (no two vertices adjacent) by size; this paper studies how often −1 is a root, called the graph's 'multiplicity.' This new invariant surprisingly connects to topology (detecting contractible independence complexes), combinatorics (implying log-concavity of coefficients), and structural graph theory, opening a fresh line of inquiry linking algebra and discrete geometry.

math.CO
Original abstract

We initiate the study of the multiplicity of negative one of independence polynomials of graphs. In this article, we simply refer to this as the \emph{multiplicity} of a graph. As applications, we provide a graph-theoretic description of trees whose independence complexes are contractible, give a new sufficient condition for independence polynomials of graphs to be log-concave, and finally, determine possible pairs $(\operatorname{mult}_{-1}P_G, α(G))$, where $P_G$ denotes the independence polynomial of $G$, and $α(G)$ the independence number. The study of the pairs $(\operatorname{mult}_{-1}P_G, α(G))$ is equivalent to finding all pairs of the numerator degree and denominator degree of the Hilbert series of the edge ideal of $G$. We also use spectral graph theory to obtain results on the multiplicity of line graphs of forests. Finally, we give some translations and applications in combinatorial commutative algebra.

Palindromic Poincaré polynomials in Type $B$

Gaston Burrull

The Poincaré polynomial of a group element encodes how many group elements sit below it in a natural ordering (the Bruhat order). This paper completely classifies which Poincaré polynomials are palindromic—equal when read forwards or backwards—in the hyperoctahedral groups (type B), and shows that all combinatorial data from the symmetric groups (type A) embeds into type B. This settles a natural structural question and transfers results about Kazhdan-Lusztig polynomials, key objects in representation theory, between these families.

math.CO math.RT
Original abstract

We completely describe the set of all palindromic Poincaré polynomials in type $B_n$. Additionally, we show that all lower Bruhat intervals in type $A_n$ occur in type $B_n$. As a consequence, all Poincaré polynomials $P_w(q)$ and all Kazhdan--Lusztig polynomials $P_{x,y}(q)$ of type $A_n$ occur in type $B_n$.

Newton polygons for the non-bipartite dimer model

Vladimir Bošković

The dimer model counts perfect matchings on graphs, and its Newton polygon—a convex lattice polygon encoding statistical mechanical data—is well-understood for bipartite graphs. This paper tackles two natural non-bipartite constructions and shows precisely how their Newton polygons relate to the underlying bipartite ones. The key bonus: the polygon's edge directions are identified with homology classes of zig-zag paths, giving a clean geometric interpretation previously available only in the bipartite setting.

math.CO math-ph
Original abstract

We study the dimer model on two families of non-bipartite graphs on a torus. The first family is obtained by replacing degree $3$ vertices in a bipartite torus graph with triangles, while the second consists of corner graphs associated with bipartite torus graphs. We determine the relationship between the Newton polygons of these graphs and those of the underlying bipartite graphs. We also identify the primitive edge vectors of the Newton polygons with the homology classes of the zig-zag paths. We further consider the marginal polynomials obtained by restricting to monomials corresponding to a boundary side of the Newton polygon. For the triangular lattice and the Fisher graph of the hexagonal lattice, we prove that these polynomials are real-rooted and obtain an explicit factorization of their roots. Finally, we introduce new local moves, including a move on non-planar graphs, that preserve the dimer partition functions up to a scale.

A Spectral Confirmation of the Erdős Matching Conjecture

Liying Kang, Yongchun Lu, Xiying Yuan et al.

The Erdős Matching Conjecture asks how many edges an r-uniform hypergraph (a generalization of a graph where edges connect r vertices) can have if no s independent edges exist simultaneously. This paper proves the spectral version: for large n, it finds which hypergraph maximizes the largest eigenvalue of its adjacency tensor under the same constraint. Spectral extremal results often unlock purely combinatorial ones, making this a meaningful step toward fully resolving the original 1965 conjecture.

math.CO
Original abstract

The Erdős Matching Conjecture concerns the maximum number of hyperedges in an $r$-uniform hypergraph with bounded matching number. In this paper, we study a spectral counterpart of this conjecture. For sufficiently large $n$, we determine the maximum spectral radius over all $n$-vertex $r$-uniform hypergraphs whose matching number is less than $s$, and characterize the unique extremal hypergraph. To establish the main theorem, we first apply the shifting method to reduce the problem to shifted hypergraphs. We then derive several spectral upper bounds through hypergraph decomposition and related variational estimates for tensor spectral radii. With these estimates, we analyze the structural properties of shifted-saturated hypergraphs and prove the spectral extremal theorem for shifted hypergraphs with bounded matching numbers. Finally, we drop the shifted condition and extend our spectral bound to general $r$-uniform hypergraphs. Our main theorem states that for any $n$-vertex $r$-uniform hypergraph $H$ with matching number $ν(H)<s$, the inequality $ρ(H)\leq ρ(\mathcal{F}_{s-1}(n))$ holds whenever $n$ is sufficiently large. Here $\mathcal{F}_{a}(n)$ denotes the family of all $r$-subsets of $[n]$ intersecting the vertex set $[a]$, and equality is attained if and only if $H$ is isomorphic to $\mathcal{F}_{s-1}(n)$. As an immediate corollary, we derive a spectral counterpart of the classical Erdős-Ko-Rado theorem for intersecting hypergraph families.

The existence of some directed strongly regular graphs on 108 vertices

Dean Crnković, Andrea Švob, Matea Zubović Žutolija

Directed strongly regular graphs are networks where every vertex has the same number of neighbors and any two vertices share a prescribed number of common neighbors, with direction mattering. The paper constructs eleven previously unresolved examples on 108 vertices by finding finite groups that act symmetrically on 108 points and using those symmetries to build the graphs. Filling these existence gaps matters because a complete census of small directed strongly regular graphs informs both combinatorics and the design of efficient communication networks.

math.CO
Original abstract

In this paper, we prove the existence of directed strongly regular graphs with parameters (108,11,3,2,1), (108,14,10,0,2), (108,22,12,6,4), (108,23,9,8,4), (108,25,15,8,5), (108,34,18,12,10), (108,38,22,12,14), (108,39,23,14,14), (108,41,35,16,15), (108,42,33,18,15) and (108,46,22,19,20). The constructions are obtained by considering finite groups acting transitively on 108 vertices.

On Turán Number of Graphs with Small Minimum Feedback Vertex Numbers

Xiao-Chuan Liu, Xu Yang

The Turán number ex(n,H) is the maximum edges a graph on n vertices can have while containing no copy of H. This paper studies Turán numbers for bipartite graphs classified by their 'feedback vertex number' — the fewest vertices whose removal destroys all cycles. The authors establish sharp or near-sharp bounds, revealing how a graph's cycle structure, not just its size, controls extremal behavior.

math.CO
Original abstract

Given a graph $H$, the minimum feedback vertex number of $H$ is the minimum number of vertices whose removal results in an acyclic graph. In this paper, we investigate Turán-type extremal problems for bipartite graphs in terms of their feedback vertex number. Our first result concerns bipartite graphs $H$ with minimum feedback vertex number one. Such graphs can be obtained from a forest by identifying a specified collection of leaves into a single vertex. For these graphs, we show that $\text{ex}(n, H)$ is upper bounded by $O(n^{1+1/k^\ast})$, where $2k^\ast$ is the length of the shortest cycle contained in $H$. In addition, we consider a family of bipartite graphs with minimum feedback vertex number three. Let $E_{k,t}$ be the graph obtained from the theta graph $θ_{k,t}$ by joining a new vertex $x$ to one side of the bipartition and another vertex $y$ to the other. Let $E^+_{k,t}$ denote the graph obtained by adding the edge $xy$ to $E_{k,t}$. We prove that for any $k\geq 2$ and sufficiently large $t$, $\text{ex}(n, E^+_{k,t})= Θ(n^{\frac{3k-1}{2k-1}}).$

A Matching-Number Refinement of Brouwer's Laplacian Eigenvalue Inequality

Jing Huang, Chunyan Qin

math.CO
Original abstract

Let $G=(V,E)$ be a finite simple graph with Laplacian eigenvalues $λ_1(L(G))\ge\cdots\geλ_{|V|}(L(G))$, and define \[ \eps_k(G)= \sum_{j=1}^{\min\{k,|V|\}}λ_j(L(G))-|E|. \] Let $ν(G)$ be the matching number of $G$, and let $n(G)$ be the number of non-isolated vertices of $G$. Lew proved that \(\eps_k(G)\le kν(G)+\lfloor k/2\rfloor\), and conjectured that the additive term can be removed in the non-endpoint range. We prove this conjecture: \[ \eps_k(G)\le kν(G) \qquad (1\le k\le n(G)-2). \] We also characterize all equality cases. Up to isolated vertices, equality holds precisely for stars, for \(K_1\vee(K_k\cup\overline{K_{n-k-1}})\) with \(k\) odd, and for \(K_n-E(K_{1,t})\) with \(n\) odd, \(k=n-2\), and \(1\le t\le n-2\). We also analyze the endpoint range \(k\ge n(G)-1\), where \(\eps_k(G)=|E|\), and determine the specific cases where the inequality \(\varepsilon_k(G)\le kν(G)\) fails or holds with equality.

A coarse block-cut tree theorem

Júlia Baligács, Václav Blažej, Jadwiga Czyżewska et al.

Classical graph theory says every graph decomposes along cut-vertices into 2-connected pieces — but what happens in the 'coarse' setting, where you ignore fine-scale geometry? This paper proves a large-scale analogue: any graph admits a tree decomposition where pieces are 'coarsely 2-connected,' meaning no small-diameter separator can split them. This extends structural graph theory to coarse geometry, relevant to geometric group theory and metric embeddings.

math.CO cs.DM
Original abstract

We prove a coarse analogue of the classic fact that every graph can be decomposed along its cut-vertices into $2$-connected components. Precisely, we prove that for every graph $G$ and a positive integer $d$, $G$ admits a tree decomposition whose adhesion sets have weak diameter at most $3d+2$ so that no two vertices $u,v$ lying in the same bag can be separated by a set of weak diameter at most $d$ whose distance from $u$ and $v$ is more than $d$. By the Coarse Menger's Theorem for two paths, this condition admits also a dual formulation, phrased in terms of the existence of two paths that are far from each other and connect the vicinity of $u$ with the vicinity of $v$.

A coarse block-cutvertex tree-decomposition

Sandra Albrechtsen, Agelos Georgakopoulos

The classical block-cutvertex decomposition breaks a connected graph into its 2-connected pieces (blocks) arranged in a tree structure. This paper constructs a coarse version of that decomposition, grouping blocks together to produce a cruder but structurally useful tree-decomposition—likely enabling applications where exact block structure is too fine-grained.

math.CO
Original abstract

We obtain a coarse version of the block-cutvertex tree-decomposition of a connected graph.

Coloring digraphs with $Δ-b$ colors

Ken-ichi Kawarabayashi, Lucas Picasarri-Arrieta

The dichromatic number measures how many colors you need to partition a directed graph's vertices so no directed cycle is single-colored. This paper proves an analog of a classical undirected result: every digraph either contains a large dense biclique (all possible opposing arcs between two vertex sets) or can be colored with roughly √(max in-degree × out-degree) minus a fixed constant colors, tightening our understanding of directed graph coloring.

math.CO cs.DM
Original abstract

The dichromatic number of a digraph is the minimum number of colors needed to partition its vertex set into acyclic subdigraphs. A biclique is a set of vertices inducing all possible pairs of opposite arcs. For a digraph $D$, define $Δ(D) = \max_{v\in V(D)} \sqrt{d^+(v) \cdot d^-(v)}$. We prove that, for every fixed integer $b\in\mathbb{N}$, every digraph $D$ with $Δ(D) = Δ$ being sufficiently large with respect to $b$ either contains a biclique whose size exceeds $Δ-2b$ or has dichromatic number at most $Δ-b$. This extends a classical result of Reed to the directed setting and supports a conjecture of the present authors. Furthermore, the theorem is tight, as for all integers $b$ and $Δ\geq 3b$ there exists a digraph $D$ with $Δ(D)= Δ$, dichromatic number $Δ-b+1$, and whose largest biclique has size $Δ-2b+1$.

An Erdős-Pósa theorem for cycles and faces of distinct lengths

J. Pascal Gollin, Maximilian Gorsky, Meike Hatzel et al.

Given a graph, must it either contain many cycles of distinct lengths or have a small set of vertices whose removal kills most cycle-length diversity? This paper proves exactly that: either $k$ vertex-disjoint cycles of different lengths exist, or a 'hitting set' of size roughly $k^6$ suffices to reduce the number of distinct cycle lengths below $k$. This is an Erdős-Pósa theorem — a classical framework trading packing objects against covering them — now extended to cycle-length diversity.

math.CO cs.DM
Original abstract

We show that for every $k \in \mathbb{N}$, every graph $G$ contains $k$ vertex-disjoint cycles of different lengths, or there exists a set $X \subseteq V(G)$ with $|X| \in \mathcal{O}(k^6\mathsf{polylog}(k))$ such that $G-X$ has at most $k-1$ cycle lengths. We also prove analogous results for facial lengths of embedded graphs. Let $G$ be a graph with a closed 2-cell embedding $ψ$ on a surface $Σ$ of Euler genus $g$, let $c$ be a colouring of the faces $\mathcal{F}(ψ)$ of $ψ$, and let $R(G,ψ)$ be the radial graph of $(G, ψ)$. Then there exist $k$ faces $F_1, \ldots , F_k \in \mathcal{F}(ψ)$ that are given pairwise distinct colours by $c$ and are pairwise at distance at least $d$ in $ψ$, or there exists a set $X \subseteq V(G)$ of order at most $\mathcal{O}(k^2dg)$ such that $|\{ c(F) \mid F \in \mathcal{F}(ψ) \text{ and } V(F) \cap \bigcup_{x \in X} N^d_{R(G,ψ)}(x) = \emptyset \}| \leq k(k+2)$. Finally, using a result from additive combinatorics, we show that there are subdivided ladders with only a small number of cycle lengths. This suggests that it may be difficult to improve our bounds.

Computation of small reflective and dihedral Ramsey numbers

Ivan Damnjanović, Irena Đorđević

Ramsey theory asks: how large must a graph be to guarantee a certain substructure? This paper extends classical Ramsey numbers to a new 'permutation Ramsey' framework that unifies standard and ordered versions, then computes small examples for reflective and dihedral symmetry groups. The results provide concrete benchmarks in a recently-introduced hierarchy that interpolates between two previously separate Ramsey theories.

math.CO cs.DM
Original abstract

Throughout, all graphs are simple, finite and have vertex sets of the form $\{ 0, 1, 2, \ldots, n - 1 \}$ for some $n \in \mathbb{N}$. For graphs $G$ and $H$, and a permutation group $Γ$ on the vertex set of $H$, we say that $H$ is $Γ$-embeddable in $G$ if there exists a graph homomorphism from $H$ to $G$ of the form $ψ\circ \varphi$, where $\varphi \in Γ$ and $ψ$ is an increasing injection. Recently, standard and ordered Ramsey numbers of graphs were unified through the introduction of permutational Ramsey numbers, defined as follows. For graphs $H_1, H_2, \ldots, H_k$ and permutation groups $Γ_1, Γ_2, \ldots, Γ_k$ on their respective vertex sets, the permutational Ramsey number $R(H_1^{Γ_1}, H_2^{Γ_2}, \ldots, H_k^{Γ_k})$ is the minimum $n \in \mathbb{N}$ such that for every $k$-edge-coloring of a complete graph on $n$ vertices, there exists some $j \in \{1, 2, \ldots, k\}$ for which $H_j$ is $Γ_j$-embeddable in the spanning subgraph of the complete graph comprising the edges of color $j$. Here, we consider reflective (resp. dihedral) Ramsey numbers, which are a specific class of permutational Ramsey numbers in which each group $Γ_j$ is the reflection group (resp. dihedral group) on the naturally ordered vertex set of $H_j$. Focusing on the two-color case, we apply the SAT-based approach originally proposed by Poljak for ordered Ramsey numbers and recently extended to cyclic Ramsey numbers. We utilize the Kissat SAT solver to obtain exact values and lower bounds for small reflective and dihedral Ramsey numbers whose two arguments belong to the following graph classes: monotone and alternating paths, monotone cycles, start-central stars, complete graphs and nested matchings. We also derive several general results and formulate conjectures based on the computational findings.

Dihedral Tilings of the Sphere by Kites and Regular Polygons

Robert Barish, Kam Hang Cheng, Hoi Ping Luk

Which ways can you tile a sphere edge-to-edge using only kites and regular polygons with at least 4 sides, allowing two different tile shapes (dihedral tilings)? This paper gives a complete classification of all such tilings, using combinatorial and geometric arguments, and identifies genuinely new phenomena not seen in single-tile-type cases.

math.CO math.MG
Original abstract

In this article, we study the edge-to-edge dihedral tilings of the sphere by kites and regular $m$-gons with $m\ge 4$. All such tilings have been identified and fully classified, using various combinatorial and geometric tools. New phenomena are observed among the tilings.

All Y-friezes come from $\mathrm{SL}_2$-friezes

Ian Short, Andrei Zabolotskii

Friezes are arrays of numbers satisfying local determinant conditions; SL₂-friezes use 2×2 determinants, while Y-friezes satisfy multiplicative relations from cluster algebra theory. This paper proves every Y-frieze comes from an SL₂-frieze, settling a standing conjecture, and gives three equivalent characterizations of exactly which SL₂-friezes produce the same Y-frieze.

math.CO
Original abstract

We resolve a conjecture of de Saint Germain: all Y-friezes arise from $\mathrm{SL}_2$-friezes. Additionally, we give a threefold characterisation of those $\mathrm{SL}_2$-friezes that give rise to the same Y-frieze: an elementary characterisation using quiddities, a combinatorial characterisation using triangulated polygons, and a geometrical characterisation using chains of horocycles.

Honeycombs and Sums of Hermitian Matrices, Revisited

Ankur Moitra, Alexander Postnikov, Dora Woodruff

Given three Hermitian matrices A, B, and A+B, which combinations of their eigenvalues are possible? Knutson and Tao famously answered this using combinatorial objects called honeycombs, but the *why* remained mysterious. This paper gives an axiomatic proof: four abstract properties shared by honeycombs and Hermitian spectra force them to encode identical information, revealing the conceptual mechanism behind the correspondence rather than just verifying it computationally.

math.CO math.RT
Original abstract

We give a new proof of the celebrated theorem of Knutson and Tao that the spectra of triples $A, B, A+B$ of Hermitian matrices exactly correspond to positions of boundary rays of honeycombs. Most importantly, our proof gives new insights into why honeycombs are related to Hermitian matrices in the first place. Our proof is axiomatic: We distill four essential properties shared by honeycombs and spectra of Hermitian triples, and show that any two objects sharing these four properties must be equivalent. In this way, we argue that honeycombs are `model organisms' for Hermitian triples: they are families of objects satisfying the same defining properties, but in more obvious ways.

A Higher-Order Clique Density Theorem

Heng Li, Hong Liu, Yixiao Zhang

The clique density theorem pins down the minimum number of complete graphs K_r (cliques on r vertices) a graph must contain given its edge density. This paper proves a higher-order version: fixing the density of K_s cliques instead of edges, it determines the minimum possible K_r density for all 3≤s<r. Remarkably, the extremal graphs are still classical multipartite constructions, despite the constraint now being genuinely nonlinear and leaving edge density free.

math.CO
Original abstract

Reiher's clique density theorem determines the sharp lower envelope for the density of $K_r$ at fixed edge density. We prove a higher-order version in which the prescribed quantity is itself a clique density. For every $3\le s<r$, we determine the minimum possible $K_r$-density among graphons with prescribed $K_s$-density. For $s\ge3$ the constraint is genuinely nonlinear and leaves the edge density undetermined; nevertheless, on the positive range the sharp lower boundary is the classical multipartite edge-to-clique profile, reparametrised by $K_s$-density. We also prove stability on the positive branches of this profile: at every interior point, near extremality forces cut-distance closeness to the corresponding extremal family at the induced edge density.

The crumby coloring conjecture for subcubic outerplanar graphs

József Pintér

A 'crumby coloring' splits a graph's vertices into red and blue so that blue vertices have degree at most one and red vertices form no isolated vertices or length-3 paths. This conjecture, rooted in deep questions about graph coloring and planar graphs, was previously verified for 2-connected subcubic outerplanar graphs. This paper extends the result to all subcubic outerplanar graphs, including those that aren't 2-connected, completing the conjecture in this setting.

math.CO
Original abstract

The red-blue vertex partitions now known as crumby colorings originate in a conjecture of Thomassen related to Wegner's conjecture on squares of planar graphs. In such a coloring, the blue vertices induce a graph of maximum degree at most one, while the red vertices induce a graph with no isolated vertices and no simple path with three edges. Barát, Blázsik and Damásdi proved that every 2-connected outerplanar graph of maximum degree at most three admits a crumby coloring, and conjectured that the 2-connectivity assumption can be removed. We prove this conjecture: every finite simple subcubic outerplanar graph admits a crumby coloring. To prove the conjecture, we introduce a rooted grammar for subcubic outerplanar graphs. The grammar describes such graphs recursively using rooted branches and two-terminal path fragments; cyclic blocks are handled by deleting the root to obtain a path fragment. We correspondingly extend crumby colorings to crumby-admissible colorings: in a rooted branch the root, and in a path fragment the two terminals, are allowed to be temporary isolated red vertices. This relaxation makes induction along the grammar possible while retaining only finite boundary information. The induction reduces to verifying an explicit finite family of lower certificates, namely nonempty sets of boundary types and root states. The required verification has two parts: the family must be closed under all steps of the decomposition, and every certified completed branch must contain a final-legal root state, so that the temporary defect disappears and the resulting coloring is a genuine crumby coloring. This final step is computer-assisted: a stand-alone certificate checker, supplied with the paper, verifies the stated closure and crumby conditions for the supplied certificate. All structural reductions and the certificate-induction principle are proved by hand.

Minimum rank of complements of Kneser graphs

Tao Hu, Quanyu Tang

The minimum rank of a graph is the smallest rank achievable by a symmetric matrix whose zero/nonzero pattern matches the graph's edges—a key quantity in combinatorial linear algebra. This paper determines the minimum rank of the 'Johnson graph' I(n,k), where vertices are k-element subsets of {1,…,n} adjacent when they intersect, proving it equals n−2k+2 over any infinite field. This resolves an open problem from an AIM workshop, with the clean field-independent formula being a notable surprise.

math.CO
Original abstract

We determine the symmetric minimum rank of the complement of the Kneser graph $\operatorname{KG}(n,k)$ over every infinite field. More precisely, if $I(n,k)$ is the graph on the $k$-subsets of $[n]$ in which two vertices are adjacent exactly when they intersect, then $$ \operatorname{mr}^{\mathbb F}(I(n,k))=n-2k+2 $$ for every infinite field $\mathbb F$ and all integers $n,k$ with $2\le k\le n/2$. In particular, over the real numbers, this settles a question posed in the AIM workshop open questions report on spectra of families of matrices described by graphs.

Generalized spectral closedness of $\mathcal{F}$-free graph classes

Wei Wang, Quanyu Tang

Two graphs are generalized cospectral if they share the same characteristic polynomial along with their complements'—a strong spectral fingerprint. This paper develops conditions under which entire classes of graphs defined by forbidden induced subgraphs are 'generalized spectrally closed,' meaning no graph outside the class is generalized cospectral with one inside it. The key tool is a new combinatorial object called patterned closed walks, which interweaves a graph's edges with its complement's to capture structural information spectra alone miss.

math.CO
Original abstract

In this paper, we investigate the generalized spectral closedness of graph classes defined by a family $\mathcal{F}$ of forbidden induced subgraphs. To systematically study this property, we introduce a novel combinatorial concept of patterned closed walks (or $β$-closed walks), which naturally interlaces the edges of a graph with those of its complement. By establishing the induced-subgraph expansion of these $β$-closed walk counts, we obtain an algebraic sufficient condition for generalized spectral closedness based on the existence of a walk-realizable $\mathcal{F}$-supporter. Crucially, the search for such a walk-realizable supporter is reduced to a linear programming feasibility problem. As primary applications of this computational framework, we prove that the classes of threshold graphs and chain graphs are generalized spectrally closed.

Nearly tight bounds for induced subdivisions

Zach Hunter, Aleksa Milojević, Patryk Morawski et al.

A classical result guarantees that dense graphs must contain a subdivision of a complete graph—a copy where edges are replaced by paths. This paper studies the harder *induced* variant, where no extra edges are allowed, proving nearly tight bounds on how dense a graph must be to force an induced subdivision of any fixed graph H. The result essentially closes a problem left open by earlier work of Kühn and Osthus.

math.CO
Original abstract

Subdivisions of complete graphs play a central role in combinatorics, having deep connections to structural, extremal, and topological aspects of graph theory. A celebrated conjecture of Mader, proved independently by Bollobás and Thomason and by Komlós and Szemerédi, states that every graph of average degree of order $h^2$ contains a subdivision of $K_h$. In this paper, we consider the induced variant of this problem. A theorem of Kühn and Osthus implies that, for every fixed graph $H$ and every $s\ge 1$, graphs of sufficiently large average degree contain either a copy of $K_{s,s}$ or an induced subdivision of $H$. However, even for $H=K_h$, the best previous quantitative bounds were far from optimal. We prove nearly tight bounds for forcing induced subdivisions of $K_h$. We show that every $K_{s,t}$-free graph of average degree $Ω_{s,t}(h^{2(s-1)}\log^{7(s-1)} h)$ contains an induced subdivision of $K_h$, and that every $C_{2k}$-free graph with $k \geq 3$ and average degree $Ω_k(h\log^5 h)$ contains an induced subdivision of $K_h$. These bounds substantially improve the previously known results and are nearly optimal in both settings. They also hold if $K_h$ is replaced by any other graph on $h$ vertices.

On a conjecture regarding the product version of the Hilton-Milner theorem

Xucheng Bu, Lihua Feng, Zejun Huang et al.

The Hilton-Milner theorem characterizes the largest intersecting set families with no common element shared by all sets; a recent 'product version' asks instead to maximize the product of sizes of two cross-intersecting families. Frankl and Wang conjectured the maximum is achieved by natural Hilton-Milner-type configurations, and this paper both proves the conjecture in certain cases and identifies parameter ranges where it unexpectedly fails.

math.CO
Original abstract

Recently, Frankl and Wang considered a product version of the classical Hilton-Milner theorem. They conjectured that, if $\mathcal{F} \subset \binom{[n]}{k}$ and $\mathcal{G} \subset \binom{[n]}{\ell}$ are non-trivial cross-intersecting families with $n \geq 2k > 2\ell \geq 4$, the maximum of $|\mathcal{F}||\mathcal{G}|$ is attained by the natural Hilton-Milner-type configurations.
